## `0016` — Q3 resolution: distinct per-Category `subject_ref` fields + oneOf/union exclusivity (§0014 cascade fully discharged)

- **Status:** accepted
- **Date:** 2026-05-16
- **Context:** Q3 (subject reference polymorphism) was opened as cascade enactment of [§0015](#0015--q1-resolution-candidate-b-declaredsession--operationalsession--0014-cascade-trigger-fires-q3-pre-gate-opened) (Q1 resolution to Candidate B). Per [§0014's](#0014--23-redaction-plan-ontology-rfc-q1-before-charter-redaction-q3-subject-ref-polymorphism-and-q2-identity-tiers-assessed-at-23-step-11) lazy pre-Gate refinement, Q1-B's selection triggered Q3 as the next pre-§2.3 dependency. Discussion phase produced evidence ([`docs/rfcs/discussion/q3-evidence.md`](../rfcs/discussion/q3-evidence.md)) with seven findings, including a §2.2 compliance verdict distinguishing structurally-clean (B) from mechanism-conditional (A) compliance shapes, and a methodologically novel scaffold finding (post-Q1 inherited-pressure-without-inherited-lean). The discussion phase recommended Candidate B with 4 reversal conditions; the resolution phase deliberated and accepted, with three committee extensions (granularity at per-Category coarse level; exclusivity enforcement via schemas-level oneOf/union; no per-field glossary entries).
- **Decision:** The committee adopts **Candidate B** — distinct per-Category `subject_ref` fields on the Assertion entity. Three committee extensions accompany the candidate selection:
  - **Granularity: per-Category coarse (3 fields).** `subject_ref_observation` (Cat I), `subject_ref_construct` (Cat II), `subject_ref_hypothesis` (Cat III). Rationale: §2.2 separation is at Category level, not subtype level; sub-Category granularity is not warranted at the Assertion entity layer.
  - **Exclusivity enforcement: schemas-level oneOf/union with mandatory population.** Exactly one `subject_ref_X` field per Assertion; schemas validation rejects zero or more-than-one populated. Structural commitment encoded in `entity-model.md` Assertion entity prose.
  - **No per-field glossary entries.** The three fields are structural mechanism, not canonical entities. Contrast [§0015](#0015--q1-resolution-candidate-b-declaredsession--operationalsession--0014-cascade-trigger-fires-q3-pre-gate-opened) which added `declared session` and `operational session` as canonical entities (those are entity types, not reference fields).
- **Constitutional review:** No Charter invariant amended. Resolution respects [§2.1](./constitutional-charter.md#21-observational-integrity) (Cat I substrate immutability — Assertions do not violate), [§2.2](./constitutional-charter.md#22-epistemic-separation) (Cat I/II/III separation — exemplified structurally by typed-distinct fields and oneOf/union constraint), [§2.5 frozen v0.3](./constitutional-charter.md#25-hypothesis-lifecycle-explicitness) (no subject_ref references therein per F-SCAFFOLD-4 finding from [`q3-evidence.md`](../rfcs/discussion/q3-evidence.md); §2.5 is shape-agnostic on Q3 — verified empirically pre-resolution). Compliance with [§4 frozen v0.2](./constitutional-charter.md#4-constitutional-design-rule) (Constitutional Design Rule) — B's structural exclusivity-by-oneOf is §4-compliant by structural construction, mirroring project precedent established by §2.5 (state-as-projection structurally enforced), Q1-B (typed-distinct Session forms per [§0015](#0015--q1-resolution-candidate-b-declaredsession--operationalsession--0014-cascade-trigger-fires-q3-pre-gate-opened)), and Q2-A.2 (typed subtypes per [§0010](#0010--q2-resolution-hypothesis-subtypes-as-distinct-types-under-abstract-hypothesis)).
- **Consequences:**
  - [`docs/ontology/entity-model.md`](../ontology/entity-model.md) gains a new "The Assertion Type and Cross-Category References" section (parallel to the Category I/II/III sections; introduces the three typed reference fields, the exclusivity invariant, and the per-Category granularity rationale). §Open Modeling Question 2 closed; §Resolved Modeling Questions gains a Q3 entry. Status banner reflects Q3 resolution alongside Q2 ([§0010](#0010--q2-resolution-hypothesis-subtypes-as-distinct-types-under-abstract-hypothesis)) and Q1 ([§0015](#0015--q1-resolution-candidate-b-declaredsession--operationalsession--0014-cascade-trigger-fires-q3-pre-gate-opened)) resolutions.
  - [`docs/ontology/provenance-model.md`](../ontology/provenance-model.md) §Observational Provenance and §Inferential Provenance refined to acknowledge typed-by-category subject_ref edges; substantive §Inferential Provenance content remains pending §2.4 binding text.
  - [`docs/glossary.md`](../glossary.md) unchanged (per committee decision — no per-field glossary entries warranted; the three fields are structural mechanism, not canonical entities).
  - [`.claude/CLAUDE.md` §4 status table narrative paragraph](../../.claude/CLAUDE.md) updated to record §0014 cascade full discharge (§2.3 redaction now unblocked at Step 1.1 onward).
  - **§0014 cascade fully discharges.** [§0015](#0015--q1-resolution-candidate-b-declaredsession--operationalsession--0014-cascade-trigger-fires-q3-pre-gate-opened) triggered Q3; this entry resolves. No further cascades anticipated before §2.3 redaction. The Q3 RFC moves from `discussion` to `accepted`. [§0014's](#0014--23-redaction-plan-ontology-rfc-q1-before-charter-redaction-q3-subject-ref-polymorphism-and-q2-identity-tiers-assessed-at-23-step-11) "lazy pre-Gate" methodology is operatively complete for the §2.3 pre-Gate sequence.
  - **§2.3 redaction begins Step 1.1.** The empirical assessment of Identity tiers (remaining Open Modeling Question 1 post-renumbering) and any other pending modeling questions for §2.3 binding text accommodation now occurs at Step 1.1 per §0014's design. Identity tiers remains forward-referenceable per [§0011](#0011--q4-resolution-staged-combination-demotion-criterion-layer-a--deferred-layer-b) precedent.
  - **Methodological observation 1 — Cascade enactment as a documented pattern.** [§0014](#0014--23-redaction-plan-ontology-rfc-q1-before-charter-redaction-q3-subject-ref-polymorphism-and-q2-identity-tiers-assessed-at-23-step-11) anticipated cascade triggers; [§0015](#0015--q1-resolution-candidate-b-declaredsession--operationalsession--0014-cascade-trigger-fires-q3-pre-gate-opened) enacted Q1 → Q3 cascade; §0016 resolves and discharges. The full cycle (anticipation → trigger fire → enactment → resolution → discharge) is now empirically documented. Future redactions with pre-Gate sequences may rely on the pattern: lazy pre-Gate opens minimal RFCs; cascade triggers fire as resolutions create new blocking conditions; cascade enactment happens within the triggering resolution's commit; the chain completes when no further triggers fire.
  - **Methodological observation 2 — Post-resolution scaffold state: inherited-pressure-without-inherited-lean.** Q3-1 Phase 2 surfaced a methodologically novel fourth scaffold-finding state, refining [§0015 obs 3](#0015--q1-resolution-candidate-b-declaredsession--operationalsession--0014-cascade-trigger-fires-q3-pre-gate-opened). Three pre-resolution scaffold states (implicit-lean, explicit-defer, agnostic) plus one post-resolution scaffold state (inherited-pressure-without-inherited-lean): post-Q1 scaffold remained content-agnostic on Q3, but Q1-B's resolution created external rational pressure toward Q3-B via the typed-distinctness precedent. The distinction between scaffold-content-leans and external-reasoning-creates-pressure is non-trivial; future post-resolution scaffold checks (during §2.4 / §2.6 pre-Gate sequences) may exhibit similar patterns.
  - **Methodological observation 3 — Candidate-level convergence as the third Ontology-RFC convergence shape pattern empirically observed.** [§0010](#0010--q2-resolution-hypothesis-subtypes-as-distinct-types-under-abstract-hypothesis) produced candidate-level convergence (Candidate A.2). [§0011](#0011--q4-resolution-staged-combination-demotion-criterion-layer-a--deferred-layer-b) produced form-level convergence (staged-combination). [§0015](#0015--q1-resolution-candidate-b-declaredsession--operationalsession--0014-cascade-trigger-fires-q3-pre-gate-opened) produced meta-commitment-level convergence (both candidates required the same determinism commitment). Q3 produces candidate-level again (toward B), differing in granularity from Q2 (Q2 was candidate-level intra-Category; Q3 is candidate-level cross-Category). Three shapes empirically validated; future Ontology RFCs may exhibit any.
  - **Methodological observation 4 — Committee-extension precedent extended further.** [§0010](#0010--q2-resolution-hypothesis-subtypes-as-distinct-types-under-abstract-hypothesis) introduced sub-resolution (A.2). [§0011](#0011--q4-resolution-staged-combination-demotion-criterion-layer-a--deferred-layer-b) introduced AND-composition over the form. [§0015](#0015--q1-resolution-candidate-b-declaredsession--operationalsession--0014-cascade-trigger-fires-q3-pre-gate-opened) introduced two extensions (determinism commitment; identity-tier consistency default). §0016 introduces three extensions (granularity; exclusivity enforcement; no glossary). The pattern is consistent: discussion phase proposes the form; resolution phase refines structurally per discussion findings. The committee-extension count is not the measure; the structural rigor of grounding extensions in discussion findings is.
  - **Carry-forward — Identity tiers (Open Modeling Question 1 post-renumbering) remains forward-referenceable** per [§0011](#0011--q4-resolution-staged-combination-demotion-criterion-layer-a--deferred-layer-b) precedent; §2.3 binding text accommodates per [§0015's](#0015--q1-resolution-candidate-b-declaredsession--operationalsession--0014-cascade-trigger-fires-q3-pre-gate-opened) identity-tier consistency default until Identity tiers is formally resolved.
- **Supersession:** none.

## `0017` — Gate §2.3: second object-level invariant redaction (path a, full redaction; amendment v0.4)

- **Status:** accepted.
- **Date:** 2026-05-18.
- **Context:** Gate §2.3 was the third committee-mode Charter redaction (first: §4 in Gate 1 per [§0007](#0007--gate-1-pilot-4-committee-mode-redaction-path-c-narrow-but-not-minimal); second: §2.5 in Gate §2.5 per [§0013](#0013--gate-25-first-object-level-invariant-redaction-path-a-full-redaction-amendment-v03); third: this Gate). First application of the `invariant-redactor` procedure to an object-level invariant inheriting substantially from prior frozen Charter sections (§2.1 substrate immutability + §2.5 v0.3 Cat III lifecycle event commit semantics) AND from pre-Gate Ontology RFC resolutions ([§0015](#0015--q1-resolution-candidate-b-declaredsession--operationalsession--0014-cascade-trigger-fires-q3-pre-gate-opened) Q1; [§0016](#0016--q3-resolution-distinct-per-category-subject_ref-fields--oneofunion-exclusivity-0014-cascade-fully-discharged) Q3). The Gate proceeded through Steps 1.1–1.4 across four prior commits, preceded by §2.3 prep gate ([§0014](#0014--23-redaction-plan-ontology-rfc-q1-before-charter-redaction-q3-subject-ref-polymorphism-and-q2-identity-tiers-assessed-at-23-step-11)), Q1 resolution ([§0015](#0015--q1-resolution-candidate-b-declaredsession--operationalsession--0014-cascade-trigger-fires-q3-pre-gate-opened)), Q3 resolution ([§0016](#0016--q3-resolution-distinct-per-category-subject_ref-fields--oneofunion-exclusivity-0014-cascade-fully-discharged)). The pilot tested whether the §2.5-validated procedure scales to inheritance-dominant substrate-touching content.

- **Decision:** Charter amendment v0.4 enacts §2.3 redaction. Binding text codifies multi-category provenance traversal as the structural commitment connecting §2.1 + §2.5 + Q1 + Q3 + [`provenance-model.md`](../ontology/provenance-model.md) post-Q3 typed-by-category edges into a single provenance invariant. Seven anti-patterns enumerated (AP1 orphan typed-reference, AP2 multi-populated `subject_ref`, AP3 aggregation destroying Cat II transit, AP4 cache-as-projection inversion, AP5 multi-category chain rupture, AP6 retroactive provenance rewriting, AP7 lifecycle event reference fabrication). Six boundary conditions including the multi-category-traversal observation as BC5 (Phase A Resolution 3) and the Q2 forward-reference contract as BC4 (Phase A Resolution 4 — adapted Ontology-level marker form). Skill [`epistemic-separator`](../../.claude/skills/epistemic/epistemic-separator/SKILL.md) §4 gains new forbidden construction citing §2.3 frozen v0.4 (bidirectional mutual reinforcement restored per Phase A Resolution 5).

- **Constitutional review:** No frozen invariant amended in identity-changing form. §2.1 + §2.2 + §2.5 inheritance is structural, not modifying. The Q1-B + Q3-B Ontology commitments applied to binding text via explicit inheritance citations. Q2 (Identity tiers — Open Modeling Question 1) forward-reference contract follows §2 L41 + §2.5 Layer B precedents adapted for Ontology-level pending content per Phase A Resolution 4 marker form. All binding sentences pass [`falsifiability-check`](../../.claude/skills/epistemic/falsifiability-check/SKILL.md) per Phase B per-sentence self-test (19 Pass + 5 Pass-with-caveat + 0 Fail across 24 substantive cells; caveats tracked as structural-form properties — forward-references per Resolution 4 and foundational prose per §2.x Rationale subsection precedent — not falsifiability defects).

- **Consequences:**
  - §2.3 status moves from `pending` to `frozen`. Charter has 4 frozen object-level invariants (§2.1, §2.2, §2.3, §2.5) plus §1 Thesis, §2 header, §4 frozen.
  - Glossary unchanged (no new canonical terms; Step 1.3 backlog review confirmed empty).
  - Ontology citation chain refreshed: [`provenance-model.md`](../ontology/provenance-model.md) Status banner + TODO L60; [`entity-model.md`](../ontology/entity-model.md) L56 qualifier removal + TODO L124; [`ontology.md`](../ontology/ontology.md) L43 table row. [`epistemic-separator` SKILL.md](../../.claude/skills/epistemic/epistemic-separator/SKILL.md) gains new §4 forbidden construction citing §2.3 frozen v0.4 + source citation refresh. [`charter-guardian` SKILL.md](../../.claude/skills/constitutional/charter-guardian/SKILL.md), [`falsifiability-check` SKILL.md](../../.claude/skills/epistemic/falsifiability-check/SKILL.md), [`ontology-keeper` SKILL.md](../../.claude/skills/ontology/ontology-keeper/SKILL.md), and [`invariant-redactor` SKILL.md](../../.claude/skills/constitutional/invariant-redactor/SKILL.md) pending-list refreshes (§2.3 moved from PENDING to FROZEN; §2.5 + §4 stale entries in `charter-guardian` also corrected per bundled cleanup approved at Phase C checkpoint).
  - Q2 (Identity tiers — [`entity-model.md` Open Modeling Question 1](../ontology/entity-model.md#open-modeling-questions)) remains forward-referenced via [§0015](#0015--q1-resolution-candidate-b-declaredsession--operationalsession--0014-cascade-trigger-fires-q3-pre-gate-opened) procedural default + Phase A Resolution 4 marker form. Schedule: Q2 formal resolution may surface during §2.4 or §2.6 pre-Gate sequences as blocking dependency, or remain forward-referenced indefinitely if no Charter section first depends substantively on Identity-tier semantics.

  - **Methodological observation 1 — Inheritance-dominant non-duplication shape.** [§2.5-1.4c](in-committee/§2.5-hypothesis-lifecycle-explicitness.md) produced 6 Both + 1 Adds + 1 Inherits + 0 Anchors-only verdicts across 8 elements. §2.3-1.4c produced 2 Adds-only (A3, A6) + 2 Both (A1, A4) + 2 Adds+Inherits (A5, B6) + 1 Both+Inherits (A2) + 5 pure-Inherits (B1, B2, B3, B4, B5) across 12 elements — aggregate counts: 7 Adds (in any combination) + 3 Anchors (in any combination) + 8 Inherits (in any combination). The shape difference is methodologically significant: object-level invariants without priors (§2.5: only §2.1 to inherit from) vs with substantial priors (§2.3: inherits from §2.1 + §2.5 + Q1 + Q3) exhibit qualitatively different Step 1.4 outcomes. §2.4 and §2.6 will inherit from more priors still (§2.4 from §2.1 + §2.3 + §2.5 + Q1 + Q3; §2.6 from §2.1 + §2.3 + §2.4 + §2.5) and may exhibit even higher inheritance counts. Precedent recorded: Step 1.4 verdict mix is a function of priors, not procedure. Recorded as a methodological note in [`invariant-redactor` Step 8](../../.claude/skills/constitutional/invariant-redactor/SKILL.md).

  - **Methodological observation 2 — Forward-reference contract extended to pending Ontology RFCs/open questions.** §2 L41 forward-referenced §4 (Charter sibling, originally pending). [§2.5](#0013--gate-25-first-object-level-invariant-redaction-path-a-full-redaction-amendment-v03) Layer B forward-referenced §2.6 + §2.4 (Charter siblings, pending) with a concrete follow-on RFC as tracking artifact. §2.3 B6 forward-references Q2 (Ontology-level Open Modeling Question 1, pending) using an adapted marker form (Phase A Resolution 4): explicit reference to `decision-log §0015` as procedural-default citation + reference to the Ontology-level open question as pending-formal-specification target. The contract extends to pending content at the Ontology level. [§0013](#0013--gate-25-first-object-level-invariant-redaction-path-a-full-redaction-amendment-v03) obs 3 (forward-reference contract as structural commitment) is refined: contract accommodates any pending project artifact in the Charter > Ontology hierarchy, with marker form adapted to the artifact's level. The [§0014](#0014--23-redaction-plan-ontology-rfc-q1-before-charter-redaction-q3-subject-ref-polymorphism-and-q2-identity-tiers-assessed-at-23-step-11) lazy-pre-Gate methodology + [§0015](#0015--q1-resolution-candidate-b-declaredsession--operationalsession--0014-cascade-trigger-fires-q3-pre-gate-opened) procedural defaults serve as tracking artifacts at decision-log level for Ontology-level forward-references; no concrete follow-on RFC required (Phase A Resolution 8 confirmed via option (ii) RFC-tracking-symmetry deliberation — opening an RFC mid-redaction would undermine the [§0014](#0014--23-redaction-plan-ontology-rfc-q1-before-charter-redaction-q3-subject-ref-polymorphism-and-q2-identity-tiers-assessed-at-23-step-11) methodology Step 1.2 Phase 2 Reading 2 accepted).

  - **Methodological observation 3 — Bidirectional mutual reinforcement restored via skill §4 new construction.** §2.5 closure left mutual reinforcement bidirectional implicitly ([`epistemic-separator` §4](../../.claude/skills/epistemic/epistemic-separator/SKILL.md) cited §2.5 for construction #2; §2.5 inherited operations the skill enumerated for Category III). §2.3's A3 (orphan-typed-reference) anti-pattern surfaced one-directional asymmetry — §2.3-original anti-pattern with no analog in skill §4. Phase D.1 Resolution 5 enacted a new skill §4 forbidden construction ("The assertion's source is contextually clear from the surrounding text") citing §2.3 frozen v0.4; bidirectional symmetry restored. Pattern: when §2.x redaction includes original anti-patterns without analog in skill §4, add a new construction citing the newly frozen invariant at Step 1.5 Phase D. Precedent for §2.4 and §2.6: their redactions may surface original anti-patterns requiring analogous skill §4 additions; the Step 1.5 Phase D workflow accommodates this.

  - **Methodological observation 4 — Pre-redaction boundary content accelerates Step 1.4 Phase 2.** [§2.5-1.4b](in-committee/§2.5-hypothesis-lifecycle-explicitness.md) Phase 2 authored 5 boundary conditions from scratch (§2.5 stub had no boundary preview language). §2.3-1.4b Phase 2 had 2 pre-redaction candidates surfacing in prior steps: A6 stub boundary preview (Step 1.1 inventory) + Step 1.3 multi-category-traversal methodological observation (BC5 candidate). 4 of 6 boundaries were authored at Step 1.4; 2 were verified-and-refined from pre-redaction material. Pattern: stubs with boundary previews + Step 1.3 methodological observations shorten Step 1.4 Phase 2 by providing partial pre-coverage. §2.4 stub already contains "Distinct from §2.3" boundary preview language — now superseded by §2.3 frozen v0.4 (§2.3 binding text BC1 codifies the boundary from §2.3's side; §2.4 binding text will codify from §2.4's side at its Gate). §2.6 stub may contain analogous pre-redaction boundary content per Charter §2 L41 forward-reference convention.

  - **Carry-forwards from §2.3 redaction:**
    - Q2 (Identity tiers — [`entity-model.md` Open Modeling Question 1](../ontology/entity-model.md#open-modeling-questions)) remains forward-referenced per Resolution 4 marker form + [§0015](#0015--q1-resolution-candidate-b-declaredsession--operationalsession--0014-cascade-trigger-fires-q3-pre-gate-opened) procedural default.
    - Layer B follow-on RFC (per [§0011](#0011--q4-resolution-staged-combination-demotion-criterion-layer-a--deferred-layer-b)) remains on hold pending §2.4 + §2.6 redactions. Not affected by §2.3.
    - No new RFCs opened by this redaction (Phase A Resolution 8 confirmed via option (ii) RFC-tracking-symmetry deliberation).

- **Supersession:** none.

## `0018` — Fix frozen-section parser to accept amendment-qualified status cells (v0.4.1)

- **Status:** accepted.
- **Date:** 2026-05-18.
- **Context:** [§0017](#0017--gate-23-second-object-level-invariant-redaction-path-a-full-redaction-amendment-v04) Gate §2.3 closure Phase F.4 final summary reported a discrepancy: hook self-test output `frozen ranges: 5 range(s)` when expected count was 7 (§1 + §2 + §2.1 + §2.2 + §2.3 + §2.5 + §4). Investigation traced the gap to `.claude/hooks/_parse_watchlists.py:54`'s regex `\|\s*frozen\s*\|`, which requires the status cell to contain only `frozen` with optional surrounding whitespace. Cells carrying the `frozen — minor amendment vN.Y` qualifier — introduced at amendment v0.3 ([§0013](#0013--gate-25-first-object-level-invariant-redaction-path-a-full-redaction-amendment-v03)) — silently fail to match. Consequence: §2.5 (frozen v0.3) and §2.3 (frozen v0.4) were not recognized by the parser despite their CLAUDE.md table rows correctly marking them frozen. §2.5 has been silently un-recognized by the frozen-section parser since 2026-05-15 (~3 days). Functional protection was nonetheless preserved by the §2 outer range (the `§2` row uses bare `frozen` without qualifier, so the parser recognizes it; its computed range wraps all §2.x sub-sections at lines 32:206 of current Charter). The gap is therefore cosmetic and audit-trail-relevant rather than enforcement-relevant — but still worth fixing.
- **Decision:** Patch amendment v0.4.1 enacts the parser fix. Single-character regex change: `\|\s*frozen\s*\|` → `\|\s*frozen[^|]*\|`. The new pattern matches status cells beginning with `frozen` followed by any non-pipe content (the qualifier `— minor amendment vN.Y` or any future qualifier convention). Charter banner advances v0.4 → v0.4.1; status line gets a patch-amendment clause referencing this entry. No Charter prose amended.
- **Constitutional review:** No frozen invariant amended in content. The amendment clarifies the mechanical enforcement of a parser predicate already established at Phase 4 setup. Patch-level amendment per [`amendments.md` Step 5](./amendments.md); precedent is v0.2.1 ([§0012](#0012--extend-mechanical-charter-blockquote-exemption-to-vocabulary-drift-v021)) hook-correctness fix with the same shape (parser regex change; no Charter prose amended). The fix passes [§4 frozen v0.2](./constitutional-charter.md#4-constitutional-design-rule) falsifiability discipline: the new predicate is structurally falsifiable (cells beginning with `frozen` match; other cells do not), detectable mechanically without subjective judgment.
- **Consequences:**
  - Hook self-test now reports `frozen ranges: 7 range(s)` (was 5). §2.3 and §2.5 gain explicit own-row protection at the parser level. The §2 outer range continues to provide additional defensive coverage — no semantics change for currently-staged edits.
  - **Methodological observation 1 — Self-test output as audit surface.** [§0017](#0017--gate-23-second-object-level-invariant-redaction-path-a-full-redaction-amendment-v04) Phase F.4 demonstrated the value of treating self-test count discrepancies as authoritative signals warranting investigation. The Gate §2.3 closure surfaced this bug; the patch fixes it. Pattern: self-test counts deviating from prompt expectations should be investigated before dismissing as cosmetic, even when functional protection is incidentally preserved.
  - **Methodological observation 2 — Patch-amendment ceremony for hook-correctness fixes is the established shape.** v0.2.1 ([§0012](#0012--extend-mechanical-charter-blockquote-exemption-to-vocabulary-drift-v021)) and v0.4.1 share structure: a single mechanical correction to parser logic, no Charter prose amended, ceremony preserved for audit-trail explicitness. Future hook-correctness fixes that surface during gate closures should follow the same pattern. The ceremony cost is small (RFC + amendment entry + decision-log entry + Charter banner bump) and proportional to the registrability benefit.
  - **Methodological observation 3 — Qualifier-tolerant matching as a forward design choice.** The chosen regex (`frozen[^|]*`) admits any future qualifier wording without further parser updates. Alternative tighter regexes (e.g., `frozen(\s+—\s+minor amendment v[0-9.]+)?`) would couple the parser to specific qualifier wording and require updates whenever new qualifier conventions emerge. The qualifier-tolerant form is the lower-maintenance choice.
  - **Latent risk reduced.** If future Charter structure changes ever create a configuration where the §2 outer range no longer wraps every frozen §2.x (e.g., a hypothetical §2.7 added pending while §2.3/§2.5 remain frozen), the per-section row recognition is now in place and continues to enforce. Pre-fix, the configuration would have left §2.3 and §2.5 silently unprotected.
- **Supersession:** the strict-cell-match regex established at Phase 4 setup is superseded by the qualifier-tolerant form. The setup-Phase-4 framing (when no `frozen — minor amendment vN.Y` qualifiers existed yet) was internally consistent at the time; the convention introduced at v0.3 changed the table grammar but the parser was not concurrently updated. v0.4.1 corrects the lag.

## `0019` — §2.4 redaction plan: provenance-model OMQ #2 (Decay of influence) opened as pre-Gate; OMQ #3 + Q2 + §2.3 BC1 inheritance assessed at §2.4 Step 1.1; Layer B activation anticipated

- **Status:** accepted.
- **Date:** 2026-05-18.
- **Context:** Per [`§0008`](#0008--redaction-order-for-pending-invariants-25--23--24--26-gate-1-carry-forwards-enacted), redaction order is §2.5 → §2.3 → §2.4 → §2.6. §2.5 closed at [`§0013`](#0013--gate-25-first-object-level-invariant-redaction-path-a-full-redaction-amendment-v03); §2.3 closed at [`§0017`](#0017--gate-23-second-object-level-invariant-redaction-path-a-full-redaction-amendment-v04). §2.4 (Inferential Influence Disclosure) is next. Strategic analysis surfaced that §2.4 binding text depends on [`provenance-model.md`](../ontology/provenance-model.md) Open Modeling Question 2 (Decay of influence): §2.4 governs the structural form of `influenced_by` edges, and whether decay is part of the structural commitment or operational is operative for binding text shape.

  Three other open questions or carry-forwards touch §2.4 with varying acuity:

  - **provenance-model.md OMQ #1 (Granularity of derivation)** is primarily §2.3 territory; §2.4 inherits but does not drive. Forward-referenceable per [`§0011`](#0011--q4-resolution-staged-combination-demotion-criterion-layer-a--deferred-layer-b) + [`§0017`](#0017--gate-23-second-object-level-invariant-redaction-path-a-full-redaction-amendment-v04) precedents.
  - **provenance-model.md OMQ #3 (Influence at projection vs substrate)** touches §2.4 acutely — *when* influence edges are generated affects §2.4 binding text. Coupling to OMQ #2 outcome: low under Candidate A (permanent); high under Candidate C (supersession). May surface as blocking depending on OMQ #2 outcome; assessed empirically at §2.4 Step 1.1.
  - **provenance-model.md OMQ #4 (Cross-domain provenance)** likely §3 (Non-Goals) territory; not a §2.4 blocker.
  - **entity-model.md Q2 (Identity tiers)** remains forward-referenced per [`§0017`](#0017--gate-23-second-object-level-invariant-redaction-path-a-full-redaction-amendment-v04) Resolution 4 marker form. §2.4 may surface Q2 as blocking (if inferential-influence references depend substantively on identity-tier semantics) or accept the forward-reference contract continuing.
  - **Layer B follow-on RFC** ([`ontology-revision-layer-b-deep-criterion`](../rfcs/draft/ontology-revision-layer-b-deep-criterion.md), on hold per [`§0011`](#0011--q4-resolution-staged-combination-demotion-criterion-layer-a--deferred-layer-b)) pending §2.4 + §2.6 redactions. §2.4 redaction activates Layer B's deferred falsifiability obligation per §2.5 Layer B forward-reference contract.
  - **§2.3 BC1 inheritance:** §2.3 codified `subject_ref_construct` / `subject_ref_hypothesis` edges as observational-provenance transit; §2.4 governs the inferential semantics of the same edges. §2.4 binding text inherits BC1 from §2.3's side and codifies the §2.4 side of the mutual scope statement.

- **Decision:** §2.4 redaction is preceded by OMQ #2 resolution (sequential pre-Gate). OMQ #2 RFC is opened at `discussion` status as [`ontology-revision-omq2-decay-of-influence`](../rfcs/draft/ontology-revision-omq2-decay-of-influence.md). The other open questions and carry-forwards are NOT opened as pre-Gates now; their blocking status will be assessed empirically at §2.4 Step 1.1 (anchor inventory) per [`§0014`](#0014--23-redaction-plan-ontology-rfc-q1-before-charter-redaction-q3-subject-ref-polymorphism-and-q2-identity-tiers-assessed-at-23-step-11) lazy pre-Gate methodology (validated empirically by [`§0015`](#0015--q1-resolution-candidate-b-declaredsession--operationalsession--0014-cascade-trigger-fires-q3-pre-gate-opened)→[`§0016`](#0016--q3-resolution-distinct-per-category-subject_ref-fields--oneofunion-exclusivity-0014-cascade-fully-discharged) cascade for §2.3 redaction).

  Specifically:
  - **OMQ #1 (Granularity):** Forward-referenceable per §2.3 inheritance pattern. §2.3 frozen v0.4 anchors observational-provenance structure; §2.4 inherits and does not drive OMQ #1.
  - **OMQ #3 (Influence at projection vs substrate):** Blocking status depends on OMQ #2 outcome. Assessed at §2.4 Step 1.1 after OMQ #2 resolution. If blocking, opens as cascade per [`§0014`](#0014--23-redaction-plan-ontology-rfc-q1-before-charter-redaction-q3-subject-ref-polymorphism-and-q2-identity-tiers-assessed-at-23-step-11) methodology (analogous to Q3 cascade from Q1 resolution per [`§0015`](#0015--q1-resolution-candidate-b-declaredsession--operationalsession--0014-cascade-trigger-fires-q3-pre-gate-opened) → [`§0016`](#0016--q3-resolution-distinct-per-category-subject_ref-fields--oneofunion-exclusivity-0014-cascade-fully-discharged)).
  - **Q2 (Identity tiers):** Forward-referenced per [`§0017`](#0017--gate-23-second-object-level-invariant-redaction-path-a-full-redaction-amendment-v04) Resolution 4 marker form unless §2.4 Step 1.1 finds binding-text dependency that cannot be encoded via [`§0015`](#0015--q1-resolution-candidate-b-declaredsession--operationalsession--0014-cascade-trigger-fires-q3-pre-gate-opened) procedural default.
  - **Layer B activation:** §2.4 binding text will encode Layer B's forward-reference contract reconciliation per §2.5 Layer B precedent. Layer B follow-on RFC remains on hold post-§2.4 pending §2.6 redaction.
  - **§2.3 BC1 inheritance:** §2.4 binding text codifies the §2.4 side of the mutual scope statement. No pre-Gate RFC needed; inheritance citation is structural.

- **Constitutional review:** No Charter invariant amended. The decision sets calendar discipline. The precedent for sequenced pre-Gate resolution is [`§0009`](#0009--25-redaction-plan-ontology-rfcs-q2--q4-before-charter-redaction) (§2.5 prep) + [`§0014`](#0014--23-redaction-plan-ontology-rfc-q1-before-charter-redaction-q3-subject-ref-polymorphism-and-q2-identity-tiers-assessed-at-23-step-11) (§2.3 prep, lazy pre-Gate refinement) + [`§0015`](#0015--q1-resolution-candidate-b-declaredsession--operationalsession--0014-cascade-trigger-fires-q3-pre-gate-opened) (Q1 cascade trigger) + [`§0016`](#0016--q3-resolution-distinct-per-category-subject_ref-fields--oneofunion-exclusivity-0014-cascade-fully-discharged) (Q3 cascade discharge) + [`§0017`](#0017--gate-23-second-object-level-invariant-redaction-path-a-full-redaction-amendment-v04) (§2.3 closure, forward-reference contract extension to Ontology-level pending content). This entry extends the pattern by opening one provenance-model OMQ at discussion and deferring three others + one entity-model carry-forward + one frozen-Charter-section inheritance to §2.4 Step 1.1 empirical assessment.

- **Consequences:**
  - One RFC opened: [`ontology-revision-omq2-decay-of-influence`](../rfcs/draft/ontology-revision-omq2-decay-of-influence.md) at `discussion`. Three candidates (A permanent, B operational decay parameter, C decay via lifecycle event supersession) + Candidate D (runtime classification) explicitly rejected per Q1/Q3 Candidate C rejection precedent.
  - §2.4 redaction begins after OMQ #2 resolution (estimated 2 prompts: OMQ #2 discussion → OMQ #2 resolution).
  - §2.4 Step 1.1 anchor inventory includes empirical assessment of blocking status for OMQ #3 (likely cascade trigger depending on OMQ #2 outcome), Q2 (forward-reference status continues by default), and Layer B activation reconciliation. The assessment is a discrete sub-task within Step 1.1 with explicit deliverables.
  - **Methodological observation 1 — Refining [`§0017`](#0017--gate-23-second-object-level-invariant-redaction-path-a-full-redaction-amendment-v04) Methodological Observation 2.** [`§0017`](#0017--gate-23-second-object-level-invariant-redaction-path-a-full-redaction-amendment-v04) extended forward-reference contract to pending Ontology RFCs/open questions with adapted marker form. This entry applies the methodology at the §2.4 pre-Gate level: pre-Gates opened minimally (one OMQ at discussion), other open questions assessed empirically at Step 1.1 per [`§0014`](#0014--23-redaction-plan-ontology-rfc-q1-before-charter-redaction-q3-subject-ref-polymorphism-and-q2-identity-tiers-assessed-at-23-step-11) lazy methodology. The cascade-trigger pattern ([`§0015`](#0015--q1-resolution-candidate-b-declaredsession--operationalsession--0014-cascade-trigger-fires-q3-pre-gate-opened) → [`§0016`](#0016--q3-resolution-distinct-per-category-subject_ref-fields--oneofunion-exclusivity-0014-cascade-fully-discharged)) is anticipated for OMQ #3 depending on OMQ #2 outcome. Future redactions (§2.6 prep, follow-on Charter sections) can rely on the pattern: pre-Gate prep opens minimal RFCs; cascade triggers fire as resolutions create blocking conditions; cascade enactment in a single commit captures resolution + RFC opening.
  - **Methodological observation 2 — §2.3 BC1 inheritance is structural, not procedural.** §2.4 binding text inherits BC1 from §2.3 side. The inheritance is structural (same `subject_ref_*` edges; different semantic layer — §2.3 observational, §2.4 inferential). No pre-Gate RFC; inheritance citation in §2.4 binding text at Step 1.5 Phase B. This is the first §2.x redaction where a *frozen sibling section's Boundary Condition* is inherited as load-bearing structural commitment by the next §2.x in sequence. Pattern: §2.4 (pending) inherits §2.3 BC1; §2.6 (pending) will inherit §2.5 BC5 (lifecycle-event-as-Cat-I-record) and possibly §2.4 BCs once §2.4 freezes. Cross-section BC inheritance is a structural mechanism distinct from intra-section element inheritance.
  - **Methodological observation 3 — Layer B follow-on RFC reconciliation deferred to §2.4 binding text.** §2.4 redaction activates Layer B's deferred falsifiability obligation per §2.5 Layer B forward-reference contract. §2.4 binding text encodes the reconciliation — Layer B's deep criterion specification depends on OMQ #2 outcome + §2.4's structural commitment + §2.6 future commitment. §2.4 binding text references Layer B follow-on RFC explicitly. Layer B's specification will eventually be completed post-§2.6; this RFC is the first concrete consumer of Layer B's pending status.

- **Supersession:** none.

## `0020` — OMQ #2 resolution: decay via §2.5 lifecycle event supersession (Candidate C); OMQ #3 cascade-triggered

- **Status:** accepted.
- **Date:** 2026-05-19.
- **Context:** OMQ #2 (Decay of influence — [`provenance-model.md` Open Modeling Question 2](../ontology/provenance-model.md)) was opened as §2.4 pre-Gate per [`§0019`](#0019--24-redaction-plan-provenance-model-omq-2-decay-of-influence-opened-as-pre-gate-omq-3--q2--23-bc1-inheritance-assessed-at-24-step-11-layer-b-activation-anticipated) under [`§0014`](#0014--23-redaction-plan-ontology-rfc-q1-before-charter-redaction-q3-subject-ref-polymorphism-and-q2-identity-tiers-assessed-at-23-step-11) lazy pre-Gate methodology. Discussion phase produced evidence at [`docs/rfcs/discussion/omq2-evidence.md`](../rfcs/discussion/omq2-evidence.md) with 10 numbered findings, including §2.2 compliance verdict per candidate (A clean / B-substrate clean / C clean via §2.5 inheritance), a methodologically novel Layer B feed dimension (Finding 5), and a cascade-trigger probability dimension (Finding 6 — A low / B medium / C high). The discussion phase recommended Candidate C with 4 reversal conditions; this resolution phase deliberated and accepted, with three committee extensions (B-substrate admissibility acknowledgment, no-glossary commitment, OMQ #3 cascade enactment).

- **Decision:** The committee adopts **Candidate C — decay via §2.5 lifecycle event supersession**. Three committee extensions accompany the candidate selection:

  1. **B-substrate admissibility acknowledged but dominated.** Discussion Finding 1 established B-projection collapses to rejected Candidate D shape; B-substrate is admissible-but-dominated structurally clean per §2.2 + Q1 determinism. Discussion Findings 2 / 3 / 9 / 10 established C dominates B-substrate on epistemic strength, constitutional minimalism, vocabulary cost (Finding 8), and §1 Thesis temporal-evolution support (Finding 10). B-substrate is registered as admissible-but-dominated; this is methodologically distinct from Candidate D's rejection (D is rejected as structurally incoherent; B-substrate is rejected as dominated). Pattern preserves the methodological distinction between "rejected incoherent" and "rejected dominated" for future RFCs.
  2. **No supersession-encoding glossary entry.** Supersession is read from §2.5-committed lifecycle events; not §2.4-original canonical vocabulary. Existing [`glossary`](../glossary.md) terms (`demotion`, `hypothesis lifecycle event`) suffice. Aligns with Q3-2 pattern (structural mechanism, no new entries); contrasts Q1-2 pattern (entity types added as canonical entries).
  3. **OMQ #3 cascade enactment.** Per discussion Finding 6 + [`§0014`](#0014--23-redaction-plan-ontology-rfc-q1-before-charter-redaction-q3-subject-ref-polymorphism-and-q2-identity-tiers-assessed-at-23-step-11) methodology + [`§0015`](#0015--q1-resolution-candidate-b-declaredsession--operationalsession--0014-cascade-trigger-fires-q3-pre-gate-opened)→[`§0016`](#0016--q3-resolution-distinct-per-category-subject_ref-fields--oneofunion-exclusivity-0014-cascade-fully-discharged) precedent, OMQ #3 (Influence at projection vs substrate) RFC opens at `discussion` status in this commit. OMQ #3 resolution becomes pre-Gate dependency for §2.4 redaction Step 1.1 alongside Q2 (Identity tiers) forward-reference and Layer B activation reconciliation.

- **Constitutional review:** No Charter invariant amended. Resolution respects [§2.1](../charter/constitutional-charter.md#21-observational-integrity) (Cat I substrate immutability — `influenced_by` edges committed immutably; supersession is derived projection state, not edge mutation), [§2.2](../charter/constitutional-charter.md#22-epistemic-separation) (Cat I/II/III separation — exemplified structurally by C's clean Cat I substrate edge plus Cat II projection over §2.5 lifecycle events), [§2.5 frozen v0.3](../charter/constitutional-charter.md#25-hypothesis-lifecycle-explicitness) (no modification required; F-SCAFFOLD-3 verified §2.5 Demotion subsection structurally supports Candidate C via lifecycle event consumption pattern), [§2.3 frozen v0.4](../charter/constitutional-charter.md#23-provenance-integrity) (no modification required; §2.4 binding text inherits §2.3 BC1 from §2.4 side; OMQ #2 does not affect §2.3's observational scope), and [§4 frozen v0.2](../charter/constitutional-charter.md#4-constitutional-design-rule) (C's structural exclusivity-via-§2.5-inheritance is §4-compliant by construction; supersession state structurally falsifiable as derived projection over committed substrate events).

- **Consequences:**
  - [`docs/ontology/provenance-model.md`](../ontology/provenance-model.md) §Inferential Provenance subsection revised; §Open Modeling Questions row 2 closed (moved to new §Resolved Modeling Questions); row 3 (OMQ #3) annotated with cascade trigger; Status banner refreshed.
  - [`docs/charter/decision-log.md`](./decision-log.md) entry §0020 records resolution.
  - [`docs/rfcs/draft/ontology-revision-omq2-decay-of-influence`](../rfcs/draft/ontology-revision-omq2-decay-of-influence.md) status `discussion` → `accepted`; Decision Record cites this entry plus three committee extensions.
  - [`docs/rfcs/draft/ontology-revision-omq3-influence-projection-vs-substrate`](../rfcs/draft/ontology-revision-omq3-influence-projection-vs-substrate.md) opened NEW at `discussion` status (cascade enactment per [`§0015`](#0015--q1-resolution-candidate-b-declaredsession--operationalsession--0014-cascade-trigger-fires-q3-pre-gate-opened) precedent).
  - [`docs/glossary.md`](../glossary.md) unchanged (per committee decision — no new canonical terms warranted).
  - [`.claude/CLAUDE.md`](../../.claude/CLAUDE.md) §4 status table narrative paragraph updated to record OMQ #2 resolution + OMQ #3 cascade trigger.

  - **Methodological observation 1 — Cascade-trigger probability dimension validated empirically.** OMQ #2-1 introduced cascade-probability as Phase 1 Dimension 5 (methodologically novel per [`§0014`](#0014--23-redaction-plan-ontology-rfc-q1-before-charter-redaction-q3-subject-ref-polymorphism-and-q2-identity-tiers-assessed-at-23-step-11) extension). Discussion Finding 6 confirmed high coupling under Candidate C; OMQ #2-2 fires cascade trigger as predicted. Pattern: pre-RFC analysis of cascade probability per candidate produces accurate prediction of cascade firing; future RFCs may use the dimension to anticipate cascade enactment at resolution-phase commit. First empirical validation of cascade-probability as RFC dimension; extends [`§0014`](#0014--23-redaction-plan-ontology-rfc-q1-before-charter-redaction-q3-subject-ref-polymorphism-and-q2-identity-tiers-assessed-at-23-step-11) lazy methodology by adding quantitative pre-Gate prediction.

  - **Methodological observation 2 — Layer B feed as RFC dimension.** OMQ #2-1 Phase 1 Dimension 4 introduced Layer B feed as explicit dimension (first Ontology RFC where Layer B activation is in scope; per [`§0017`](#0017--gate-23-second-object-level-invariant-redaction-path-a-full-redaction-amendment-v04) Methodological Observation 2 — forward-reference contract extension to pending Ontology RFCs). Discussion Finding 5 produced per-candidate Layer B feed shapes; Candidate C's §2.5-coupled feed (consuming existing mechanism) is structurally distinct from A's uniform-graph feed and B-substrate's parameter-coupled feed. Pattern: post-§2.3-closure RFCs whose resolutions affect Layer B's eventual specification should include Layer B feed as explicit dimension; future §2.6-touching RFCs (and Layer B follow-on RFC itself when activated post-§2.6) inherit this methodological standard.

  - **Methodological observation 3 — §2.5-sibling §2.x layering pattern extended to Ontology resolution layer.** §2.3 frozen v0.4 established sibling §2.x inheritance pattern (§2.3 inherits §2.1 immutability foundation + §2.5 lifecycle event commit semantics + Q1/Q3 Ontology commitments). OMQ #2-C extends the pattern at Ontology resolution layer: Candidate C consumes §2.5 BC5 (lifecycle-event-as-Cat-I-record) as supersession-annotation source for `influenced_by` edges. Pattern: later §2.x invariants and Ontology resolutions may consume earlier frozen §2.x structural mechanisms via Cat II projections — without retrofitting the frozen sections. Constitutional minimalism preserved. §2.6 redaction likely extends pattern further (consuming §2.3 + §2.4 + §2.5 structural mechanisms for independence computation).

  - **Methodological observation 4 — Skill §4 #4 rewrite pattern as canonical example at Ontology resolution layer.** Discussion Finding 2 surfaced that Candidate C's supersession-via-§2.5-lifecycle-event mechanism aligns exactly with [`epistemic-separator`](../../.claude/skills/epistemic/epistemic-separator/SKILL.md) skill §4 construction #4 rewrite pattern. This is bidirectional mutual reinforcement at Ontology resolution layer — skill §4 #4 pre-existed; OMQ #2-C exemplifies it; §2.4 redaction may cite skill §4 #4 explicitly per [`§0017`](#0017--gate-23-second-object-level-invariant-redaction-path-a-full-redaction-amendment-v04) Resolution 2 implicit-cross-reference precedent (or, if §2.4 binding text takes the bidirectional route, skill §4 #4 may gain explicit citation of §2.4 binding text post-§2.4-frozen). The §2.4-redaction Step 1.5 Phase A will deliberate. Pattern extends [`§0017`](#0017--gate-23-second-object-level-invariant-redaction-path-a-full-redaction-amendment-v04) Methodological Observation 3 (bidirectional mutual reinforcement via skill §4 citation) to Ontology resolution layer.

  - **Carry-forwards from OMQ #2 resolution:**
    - OMQ #3 (Influence at projection vs substrate) cascade-triggered; RFC opened at `discussion`. Resolution becomes pre-Gate dependency for §2.4 Step 1.1.
    - Q2 (Identity tiers — [`entity-model.md` Open Modeling Question 1](../ontology/entity-model.md#open-modeling-questions)) remains forward-referenced per [`§0017`](#0017--gate-23-second-object-level-invariant-redaction-path-a-full-redaction-amendment-v04) Resolution 4 marker form; not blocked by OMQ #2 resolution.

## `0021` — OMQ #3 resolution: substrate-time generation (Candidate α); §0020 cascade fully discharged

- **Status:** accepted.
- **Date:** 2026-05-19.
- **Context:** OMQ #3 (Influence at projection vs substrate — [`provenance-model.md` Open Modeling Question 3](../ontology/provenance-model.md)) was cascade-triggered by OMQ #2 resolution per [`§0020`](#0020--omq-2-resolution-decay-via-25-lifecycle-event-supersession-candidate-c-omq-3-cascade-triggered). Per [`§0014`](#0014--23-redaction-plan-ontology-rfc-q1-before-charter-redaction-q3-subject-ref-polymorphism-and-q2-identity-tiers-assessed-at-23-step-11) lazy pre-Gate methodology + [`§0015`](#0015--q1-resolution-candidate-b-declaredsession--operationalsession--0014-cascade-trigger-fires-q3-pre-gate-opened)→[`§0016`](#0016--q3-resolution-distinct-per-category-subject_ref-fields--oneofunion-exclusivity-0014-cascade-fully-discharged) cascade precedent + [`§0020`](#0020--omq-2-resolution-decay-via-25-lifecycle-event-supersession-candidate-c-omq-3-cascade-triggered) cascade-trigger probability dimension (validated empirically with high-coupling-fires-cascade prediction confirmed), OMQ #3 RFC opened at `discussion` in the OMQ #2-2 commit. Discussion phase produced evidence ([`docs/rfcs/discussion/omq3-evidence.md`](../rfcs/discussion/omq3-evidence.md)) with 7 numbered findings, including a §2.1 + §2.2 compliance verdict per candidate (α clean / β §2.1-inapplicable-by-construction + §2.2 clean conditional on Q1 explicit commitment), a methodologically novel scaffold finding (**cascade-inherited pressure** per F-SCAFFOLD-1), and a Layer B specification cost comparison (Finding 4 — α two-source consumption / β three-source consumption). The discussion phase recommended Candidate α with 5 reversal conditions and β registered as admissible-but-dominated; the resolution phase deliberated and accepted, with two committee extensions (β admissibility acknowledgment; no-§Inferential-Provenance-revision determination per F-SCAFFOLD-1 verification).

- **Decision:** The committee adopts **Candidate α — substrate-time generation**. Two committee extensions accompany the candidate selection:

  1. **β admissible-but-dominated registration.** Per [`§0020`](#0020--omq-2-resolution-decay-via-25-lifecycle-event-supersession-candidate-c-omq-3-cascade-triggered) B-substrate precedent. β is admissible under Q1 determinism explicit commitment (verified at Phase 3 epistemic-separator cell); dominated by α on cascade-inherited pressure alignment (Finding 1), §1 Thesis substrate-directness (Finding 2), Layer B specification cost (Finding 4), and §2.1 compliance clarity (α §2.1-clean; β §2.1-inapplicable-by-construction). Pattern: second instance of admissible-but-dominated registration after [`§0020`](#0020--omq-2-resolution-decay-via-25-lifecycle-event-supersession-candidate-c-omq-3-cascade-triggered) B-substrate; methodologically distinct from γ rejection (γ rejected as structurally incoherent; β rejected as dominated).
  2. **No revision to §Inferential Provenance substantive prose.** Per F-SCAFFOLD-1: [`§0020`](#0020--omq-2-resolution-decay-via-25-lifecycle-event-supersession-candidate-c-omq-3-cascade-triggered) Candidate C codification at §Inferential Provenance ("Every Assertion formed under influence carries one or more `influenced_by` edges...") is structurally α-aligned. Under α resolution, the four substantive bullets stand. Only a short acknowledgment clause was added (Phase 2.1) ratifying the substrate-side reading under §0021. Pattern: cascade-resolution may produce minimal Ontology revision when triggering resolution's codification already aligns with the cascaded resolution's direction — methodologically efficient.

- **Constitutional review:** No Charter invariant amended. Resolution respects [§2.1](../charter/constitutional-charter.md#21-observational-integrity) (Cat I substrate immutability — `influenced_by` edges committed immutably as Cat I records under α), [§2.2](../charter/constitutional-charter.md#22-epistemic-separation) (Cat I/II/III separation — α exemplifies clean Cat I edges + Cat II supersession-via-§2.5 reading), [§2.3 frozen v0.4](../charter/constitutional-charter.md#23-provenance-integrity) (no modification required; §2.4-side of BC1 inheritance shape clean under α — `influenced_by` edges are Cat I primaries directly), [§2.5 frozen v0.3](../charter/constitutional-charter.md#25-hypothesis-lifecycle-explicitness) (no modification required; [`§0020`](#0020--omq-2-resolution-decay-via-25-lifecycle-event-supersession-candidate-c-omq-3-cascade-triggered) Candidate C supersession reading consumes §2.5 lifecycle events without affecting §2.5 binding text), and [§4 frozen v0.2](../charter/constitutional-charter.md#4-constitutional-design-rule) (α's structural commitment is §4-compliant by construction; substrate-time edge generation is structurally falsifiable as Cat I event commit).

- **Consequences:**
  - [`docs/ontology/provenance-model.md`](../ontology/provenance-model.md) §Inferential Provenance acknowledgment clause added; §Open Modeling Questions row 3 closed (moved to §Resolved Modeling Questions); Status banner refreshed.
  - [`docs/charter/decision-log.md`](./decision-log.md) entry §0021 records resolution.
  - [`docs/rfcs/draft/ontology-revision-omq3-influence-projection-vs-substrate`](../rfcs/draft/ontology-revision-omq3-influence-projection-vs-substrate.md) status `discussion` → `accepted`; Decision Record cites this entry + two committee extensions.
  - [`docs/glossary.md`](../glossary.md) unchanged (no new canonical terms warranted).
  - [`.claude/CLAUDE.md`](../../.claude/CLAUDE.md) §4 status table narrative paragraph updated to record OMQ #3 resolution + §0020 cascade discharge.
  - **[`§0020`](#0020--omq-2-resolution-decay-via-25-lifecycle-event-supersession-candidate-c-omq-3-cascade-triggered) cascade fully discharges.** OMQ #2 ([`§0020`](#0020--omq-2-resolution-decay-via-25-lifecycle-event-supersession-candidate-c-omq-3-cascade-triggered)) triggered OMQ #3; OMQ #3 (this entry) resolves. No further cascades anticipated before §2.4 redaction. The two-cascade chain ([Q1→Q3 per §0015→§0016](#0015--q1-resolution-candidate-b-declaredsession--operationalsession--0014-cascade-trigger-fires-q3-pre-gate-opened); [OMQ #2→OMQ #3 per §0020→§0021](#0020--omq-2-resolution-decay-via-25-lifecycle-event-supersession-candidate-c-omq-3-cascade-triggered)) is empirically complete. [`§0014`](#0014--23-redaction-plan-ontology-rfc-q1-before-charter-redaction-q3-subject-ref-polymorphism-and-q2-identity-tiers-assessed-at-23-step-11) lazy methodology continues to extend per established pattern; future redactions can rely on cascade-probability dimension to anticipate cascade firing.

  - **Methodological observation 1 — Cascade-inherited pressure as new constitutional category.** OMQ #3-1 F-SCAFFOLD-1 surfaced a methodologically novel scaffold-finding state: cascade-enactment RFC's scaffold may inherit pressure from triggering resolution. [`§0020`](#0020--omq-2-resolution-decay-via-25-lifecycle-event-supersession-candidate-c-omq-3-cascade-triggered) Candidate C codification's "carries" vocabulary at §Inferential Provenance created α-aligned pressure on OMQ #3's scaffold; this is legitimate evidence axis given the cascade's structural coupling (OMQ #3 governs upstream of [`§0020`](#0020--omq-2-resolution-decay-via-25-lifecycle-event-supersession-candidate-c-omq-3-cascade-triggered)'s mechanism). The pressure is not illegitimate over-commitment because [`§0020`](#0020--omq-2-resolution-decay-via-25-lifecycle-event-supersession-candidate-c-omq-3-cascade-triggered)'s codification was committee-resolved with awareness of the cascade trigger. Pattern: cascade-resolution scaffolds may inherit pressure from triggering-resolution codification; pressure is decisive evidence axis when cascade coupling is structural (high coupling per [`§0020`](#0020--omq-2-resolution-decay-via-25-lifecycle-event-supersession-candidate-c-omq-3-cascade-triggered) Dimension 5 prediction). Distinct from OMQ #2-1's "inherited-pressure-without-inherited-lean" / Q3-1's post-resolution scaffold state which described scaffold pressure on a sequenced (non-cascade) RFC. Future cascade RFCs may exhibit similar inherited-pressure patterns.

  - **Methodological observation 2 — Inheritance direction in cascade trajectories.** [`§0020`](#0020--omq-2-resolution-decay-via-25-lifecycle-event-supersession-candidate-c-omq-3-cascade-triggered) Candidate C adds substrate-side expressiveness (supersession via §2.5 lifecycle event reading). OMQ #3-α consolidates substrate-side commitment without inverting (edges remain Cat I substrate). Pattern: inheritance-direction-consistency in cascades — both resolutions push toward substrate-side commitment. Future cascades may exhibit inheritance-direction-inversion (e.g., a cascade where the triggering resolution adds substrate-side expressiveness and the cascaded resolution subtracts substrate-side commitment in favor of projection-time computation). Both patterns are valid; the methodological observation is that cascade trajectories may exhibit either direction, with §1 Thesis support strongly favoring substrate-side consistency.

  - **Methodological observation 3 — Admissible-but-dominated registration pattern extends.** OMQ #2-2 B-substrate established the pattern; OMQ #3-2 β extends to second instance. Pattern increasingly established: candidates may be admissible structurally (pass §2.2 + §4 + epistemic-separator) yet dominated by an alternative on aggregate findings. Methodologically distinct from γ-style rejection (rejected as structurally incoherent). Two explicit instances now (OMQ #2-2 B-substrate; OMQ #3-2 β); the pattern enters methodological vocabulary for future RFC resolution-phase committee extensions.

  - **Methodological observation 4 — Cascade-probability dimension validated; two-cascade chain empirically complete.** [`§0020`](#0020--omq-2-resolution-decay-via-25-lifecycle-event-supersession-candidate-c-omq-3-cascade-triggered) introduced cascade-probability as Phase 1 dimension (OMQ #2-1 Dim 5). Predicted high coupling under Candidate C → cascade fires. [`§0020`](#0020--omq-2-resolution-decay-via-25-lifecycle-event-supersession-candidate-c-omq-3-cascade-triggered) enactment validated the prediction; §0021 discharge completes the cycle. Pattern: cascade-probability is now established as predictive RFC dimension; future cascade chains can be anticipated by including the dimension in discussion phase. The two-cascade chain ([Q1→Q3](#0015--q1-resolution-candidate-b-declaredsession--operationalsession--0014-cascade-trigger-fires-q3-pre-gate-opened), [OMQ #2→OMQ #3](#0020--omq-2-resolution-decay-via-25-lifecycle-event-supersession-candidate-c-omq-3-cascade-triggered)) is empirically complete; [`§0014`](#0014--23-redaction-plan-ontology-rfc-q1-before-charter-redaction-q3-subject-ref-polymorphism-and-q2-identity-tiers-assessed-at-23-step-11) lazy pre-Gate methodology continues to extend per established pattern.

  - **Carry-forwards from OMQ #3 resolution:**
    - Q2 (Identity tiers — [`entity-model.md` Open Modeling Question 1](../ontology/entity-model.md#open-modeling-questions)) remains forward-referenced per [`§0017`](#0017--gate-23-second-object-level-invariant-redaction-path-a-full-redaction-amendment-v04) Resolution 4 marker form; not blocked by OMQ #3 resolution.
    - Layer B follow-on RFC ([`ontology-revision-layer-b-deep-criterion`](../rfcs/draft/ontology-revision-layer-b-deep-criterion.md)) remains on hold per [`§0011`](#0011--q4-resolution-staged-combination-demotion-criterion-layer-a--deferred-layer-b); OMQ #2 + OMQ #3 together fully discharge Layer B's ontology.md Q5 "decaying?" axis dependency; Q5 "transitive?" axis remains open per OMQ #2-1 Finding 7.
    - [`ontology.md`](../ontology/ontology.md) §Open Questions Q5 "transitive?" half noted as second cascade candidate for §2.4 Step 1.1 per [`§0014`](#0014--23-redaction-plan-ontology-rfc-q1-before-charter-redaction-q3-subject-ref-polymorphism-and-q2-identity-tiers-assessed-at-23-step-11) lazy methodology.
    - §2.3 BC1 inheritance for §2.4 side: clean under α — §2.4-side codifies that `influenced_by` edges are Cat I substrate facts committed at formation event time; chain back to Cat I primaries via §2.3 frozen v0.4 inheritance is trivial.
    - [`provenance-model.md`](../ontology/provenance-model.md) OMQ #1 (Granularity of derivation) remains open; primarily §2.3 territory inherited; not §2.4 blocker.
    - [`provenance-model.md`](../ontology/provenance-model.md) OMQ #4 (Cross-domain provenance) remains open; likely §3 (Non-Goals) territory.

## `0022` — Implementation pivot: §6.4 amendment + §0003 reversal authorization + §2.4/§2.6 posture shift

- **Status:** accepted.
- **Date:** 2026-05-19.

- **Context:** The Charter is at v0.4.1 (this entry's commit advances it to v0.4.2 per [`amendments.md` v0.4.2`](./amendments.md)). Four object-level constitutional invariants are frozen: §2.1 Observational Integrity (v0.1), §2.2 Epistemic Separation (v0.1), §2.3 Provenance Integrity (frozen — minor amendment v0.4 per [`§0017`](#0017--gate-23-second-object-level-invariant-redaction-path-a-full-redaction-amendment-v04)), §2.5 Hypothesis Lifecycle Explicitness (frozen — minor amendment v0.3 per [`§0013`](#0013--gate-25-first-object-level-invariant-redaction-path-a-full-redaction-amendment-v03)). §1 Thesis, §2 Invariant qualification criteria, and §4 Constitutional Design Rule (frozen — minor amendment v0.2 per [`§0007`](#0007--gate-1-pilot-4-committee-mode-redaction-path-c-narrow-but-not-minimal)) are also frozen. Four full-cycle Ontology open questions are resolved: Q1 Session duality ([`§0015`](#0015--q1-resolution-candidate-b-declaredsession--operationalsession--0014-cascade-trigger-fires-q3-pre-gate-opened)), Q3 Subject ref polymorphism ([`§0016`](#0016--q3-resolution-distinct-per-category-subject_ref-fields--oneofunion-exclusivity-0014-cascade-fully-discharged)), OMQ #2 Decay of influence ([`§0020`](#0020--omq-2-resolution-decay-via-25-lifecycle-event-supersession-candidate-c-omq-3-cascade-triggered)), OMQ #3 Influence at projection vs substrate ([`§0021`](#0021--omq-3-resolution-substrate-time-generation-candidate--0020-cascade-fully-discharged)). Two empirically-complete cascade chains (Q1→Q3 per [`§0015`](#0015--q1-resolution-candidate-b-declaredsession--operationalsession--0014-cascade-trigger-fires-q3-pre-gate-opened)→[`§0016`](#0016--q3-resolution-distinct-per-category-subject_ref-fields--oneofunion-exclusivity-0014-cascade-fully-discharged); OMQ #2→OMQ #3 per [`§0020`](#0020--omq-2-resolution-decay-via-25-lifecycle-event-supersession-candidate-c-omq-3-cascade-triggered)→[`§0021`](#0021--omq-3-resolution-substrate-time-generation-candidate--0020-cascade-fully-discharged)) provide empirical validation of the lazy pre-Gate methodology established at [`§0014`](#0014--23-redaction-plan-ontology-rfc-q1-before-charter-redaction-q3-subject-ref-polymorphism-and-q2-identity-tiers-assessed-at-23-step-11). The Ontology is Drafted. Two object-level invariants remain pending: §2.4 Inferential Influence Disclosure and §2.6 Evidential Independence Integrity. §3 Non-Goals remains pending. Continued pre-implementation drafting of §2.4 and §2.6 without empirical evidence from implementation produces diminishing discriminating power — the working stubs anticipate questions about influence declaration and independence computation whose answers depend on what implementation surfaces. This entry consolidates three constitutional acts that authorize implementation against the current structural ground.

- **Decision:** The committee adopts the **implementation pivot**, consolidating three constitutional acts:

  1. **CLAUDE.md §6.4 amended (Option α — explicit inline criteria).** The prior §6.4 stub forbade implementation under any framing and referenced an `implementation-readiness-evaluator` skill "to be created in Phase 5." That skill was never created; the gate was effectively undefined. §6.4 is rewritten to operationalize the gate via three structural criteria evaluable today: (a) at least three object-level invariants frozen; (b) at least one full-cycle Ontology open question resolved; (c) the decision-log contains a pivot entry citing §6.4 by reference and authorizing implementation. The criteria are satisfied by this entry: four object-level invariants frozen (§2.1, §2.2, §2.3, §2.5), four Ontology questions resolved (Q1, Q3, OMQ #2, OMQ #3), §0022 itself satisfies (c). The gate clears once; subsequent implementation work proceeds under ordinary RFC discipline without re-clearing the gate, until and unless a future amendment re-tightens it. The "under any framing" clause is preserved structurally: the criteria are evaluable regardless of how a request is framed.

  2. **[`§0003`](#0003--defer-storage-technology-selection) reversed-by-authorization.** [`§0003`](#0003--defer-storage-technology-selection) deferred storage technology selection (along with schemas technology and implementation language) pending Charter and Ontology stabilization. The stable threshold is reached: four frozen object-level invariants + four resolved Ontology questions + Ontology Drafted. This entry authorizes subsequent technology RFCs (schemas technology, implementation language, inception-phase storage technology) to proceed under ordinary RFC discipline (status: draft → discussion → accepted per [`§0011`](#0011--q4-resolution-staged-combination-demotion-criterion-layer-a--deferred-layer-b)–[`§0021`](#0021--omq-3-resolution-substrate-time-generation-candidate--0020-cascade-fully-discharged) pattern), NOT bypassed via the pivot commit. The reversal is authorizational, not enacted: the actual technology decisions are subsequent RFCs subject to ordinary review.

  3. **§2.4 + §2.6 posture shift.** Status of §2.4 (Inferential Influence Disclosure) and §2.6 (Evidential Independence Integrity) moves from `pending committee redaction` to `pending — empirical pressure phase`. Redaction resumes when implementation work surfaces concrete questions the Charter does not already answer (regarding influence declaration semantics and evidential independence computation respectively), not on procedural cadence. The shift is recorded in the Charter banner status line, the CLAUDE.md §4 status table rows for §2.4 + §2.6, and this entry. Working stubs for both sections remain present in the Charter; the binding-text guidance ("non-binding") is unchanged. The shift records constitutional posture honestly rather than allowing the implicit "pending committee redaction" framing to mask the actual deferral mode.

- **Constitutional review:** No frozen invariant amended. No frozen-section prose modified. The Charter banner status line, CLAUDE.md §1 + §4 + §6.4, and this decision-log entry are the surface changes. The amendment is classified at patch level (v0.4.2) per the [`amendments.md` v0.2.1](./amendments.md) / [v0.4.1](./amendments.md) precedent: patches record substantive infrastructure changes whose Charter-prose touch is limited to the banner; minor (v0.5) is reserved for the next object-level invariant redaction. §6.4 amendment respects [§4 frozen v0.2](./constitutional-charter.md#4-constitutional-design-rule) falsifiability discipline — the three criteria are mechanically detectable (count frozen rows in CLAUDE.md §4 table; grep decision-log for Q/OMQ resolution entries; grep decision-log for pivot entry citing §6.4). [`§0003`](#0003--defer-storage-technology-selection) reversal is authorizational only; the technology decisions are subsequent RFCs subject to ordinary constitutional review. §2.4 + §2.6 posture shift is a status-descriptor edit; no new binding text introduced, no working stub modified beyond its categorization.

- **Consequences:**
  - [`docs/charter/constitutional-charter.md`](./constitutional-charter.md) banner refreshed (version v0.4.1 → v0.4.2; §2.4/§2.6 clause edited; v0.4.2 patch clause appended).
  - [`docs/charter/amendments.md`](./amendments.md) v0.4.2 entry appended.
  - [`.claude/CLAUDE.md`](../../.claude/CLAUDE.md) §1 refreshed (version reference + posture clause; `implementation-readiness-evaluator` noted as never-created and superseded); §4 status table rows for §2.4 + §2.6 refreshed; §4 narrative paragraph appended recording v0.4.2; §6.4 rewritten with explicit inline criteria.
  - [`docs/charter/decision-log.md`](./decision-log.md) §0022 (this entry) records the pivot; §0023 (separate entry, same commit) records Q2 (Identity tiers — Open Modeling Question 1) resolution.
  - **Subsequent technology RFCs proceed under ordinary RFC discipline.** Next commits (separate from this one): RFCs for schemas technology, implementation language, inception-phase storage technology, each at `status: draft` → committee discussion → `status: accepted` per [`§0011`](#0011--q4-resolution-staged-combination-demotion-criterion-layer-a--deferred-layer-b)–[`§0021`](#0021--omq-3-resolution-substrate-time-generation-candidate--0020-cascade-fully-discharged) pattern. After accepted, contents of `schemas/` and service skeletons follow. The pivot commit does NOT itself contain `schemas/` contents or service code; those are gated on the technology RFCs.
  - §2.4 + §2.6 redaction is deferred until empirical pressure from implementation surfaces concrete questions. The working stubs remain in the Charter (per the prior pending-committee-redaction discipline) and are non-binding until redacted.
  - Carry-forwards: Layer B follow-on RFC ([`ontology-revision-layer-b-deep-criterion`](../rfcs/draft/ontology-revision-layer-b-deep-criterion.md)) remains on hold per [`§0011`](#0011--q4-resolution-staged-combination-demotion-criterion-layer-a--deferred-layer-b) (unaffected by pivot). [`ontology.md`](../ontology/ontology.md) §Open Questions Q5 "transitive?" half remains forward-referenced per [`§0021`](#0021--omq-3-resolution-substrate-time-generation-candidate--0020-cascade-fully-discharged) Carry-forwards (unaffected by pivot). [`provenance-model.md`](../ontology/provenance-model.md) OMQ #1 (Granularity of derivation) and OMQ #4 (Cross-domain provenance) remain open per [`§0021`](#0021--omq-3-resolution-substrate-time-generation-candidate--0020-cascade-fully-discharged) (unaffected by pivot; surface when implementation requires).

- **Supersession:** [`§0003`](#0003--defer-storage-technology-selection) is partially superseded — the deferral of storage, schemas, and language technology selection is authorized to lift via subsequent RFCs (which proceed under ordinary RFC discipline). [`§0003`](#0003--defer-storage-technology-selection)'s underlying principle (technology selection is subordinate to constitutional properties) is preserved; only the deferral predicate (Charter and Ontology stable) is satisfied and the deferral lifted. The actual technology choices remain subsequent RFC commits.

## `0023` — Q2 resolution: inception-phase single-tier `actor_ref`; multi-tier formalization deferred to ordinary Ontology RFC discipline

- **Status:** accepted.
- **Date:** 2026-05-19.

- **Context:** Q2 (Identity tiers — [`entity-model.md` Open Modeling Question 1](../ontology/entity-model.md#open-modeling-questions)) is the last open question in [`entity-model.md`](../ontology/entity-model.md) §Open Modeling Questions. The question concerns formalization of the three identity tiers (`ActorRef`, `Identity`, `Cluster`) introduced informally in the entity-model discussion. Q2 has been forward-referenced through [`§0017`](#0017--gate-23-second-object-level-invariant-redaction-path-a-full-redaction-amendment-v04) Resolution 4 (§2.3 frozen v0.4 BC4 marker form — Q2 forward-reference contract extended to Ontology-level pending content), [`§0020`](#0020--omq-2-resolution-decay-via-25-lifecycle-event-supersession-candidate-c-omq-3-cascade-triggered) Carry-forwards (unblocked by OMQ #2), and [`§0021`](#0021--omq-3-resolution-substrate-time-generation-candidate--0020-cascade-fully-discharged) Carry-forwards (unblocked by OMQ #3). The implementation pivot at [`§0022`](#0022--implementation-pivot-64-amendment--0003-reversal-authorization--2426-posture-shift) supplies the occasion: subsequent technology RFCs (schemas technology, implementation language, inception-phase storage technology) and the schemas that follow require a concrete commitment on actor reference shape. This entry records that commitment.

- **Decision:** The committee adopts the following Q2 resolution shape:

  1. **Inception-phase single-tier `actor_ref` adopted.** Schemas (subsequent RFC commits per [`§0022`](#0022--implementation-pivot-64-amendment--0003-reversal-authorization--2426-posture-shift) Consequences) reference actor identity via a single string field `actor_ref` carried on Cat I primary observations (`DeclaredSession` and network-level events; per [`§0015`](#0015--q1-resolution-candidate-b-declaredsession--operationalsession--0014-cascade-trigger-fires-q3-pre-gate-opened) Q1 resolution) and propagated through Cat II constructs and Cat III hypotheses via the typed `subject_ref_*` chain (per [`§0016`](#0016--q3-resolution-distinct-per-category-subject_ref-fields--oneofunion-exclusivity-0014-cascade-fully-discharged) Q3 resolution). The single-tier reference is operationally sufficient for inception-phase ingestion + Cat II construction + Cat III hypothesis formation; cross-tier consistency semantics and identity-collision rules are not invoked at inception.

  2. **Multi-tier formalization (`ActorRef` + `Identity` + `Cluster`) deferred to ordinary Ontology RFC discipline.** The multi-tier identity model introduced informally in [`entity-model.md`](../ontology/entity-model.md) §Category I discussion remains a domain-specific concern that may proceed as ordinary Ontology evolution under ordinary RFC discipline (status: draft → discussion → accepted per [`§0011`](#0011--q4-resolution-staged-combination-demotion-criterion-layer-a--deferred-layer-b)–[`§0021`](#0021--omq-3-resolution-substrate-time-generation-candidate--0020-cascade-fully-discharged) pattern). It is NOT a Charter-level concern. The Q2 forward-reference contract at §2.3 frozen v0.4 BC4 + [`§0017`](#0017--gate-23-second-object-level-invariant-redaction-path-a-full-redaction-amendment-v04) Resolution 4 is satisfied by this resolution closing the open question. Any future identity-tier formalization is non-Charter Ontology evolution, not constitutional amendment.

  3. **Inception-phase single-tier reference is NOT a placeholder.** The single-tier `actor_ref` reference shape is operationally complete for the structural commitments §2.3 v0.4 codifies (provenance chain reconstructibility from Assertion back to Cat I primary observations). Multi-tier extension does not invalidate the inception-phase shape; multi-tier extension is supplemental (additional tiers above the base reference), not corrective. The boundary preserves §2.3 frozen text without requiring re-amendment.

- **Constitutional review:** No Charter invariant amended. No frozen-section prose modified. Q2 resolution shape respects [§2.1](./constitutional-charter.md#21-observational-integrity) (Cat I primary observations remain immutable; `actor_ref` is a content field committed at observation creation), [§2.2](./constitutional-charter.md#22-epistemic-separation) (Cat I/II/III separation preserved — `actor_ref` is content; cross-category propagation uses typed `subject_ref_*` per [`§0016`](#0016--q3-resolution-distinct-per-category-subject_ref-fields--oneofunion-exclusivity-0014-cascade-fully-discharged), not actor_ref-as-discriminator), [§2.3 frozen v0.4](./constitutional-charter.md#23-provenance-integrity) (BC4 forward-reference contract satisfied; the inception-phase single-tier shape supplies the identity-tier consistency-across-types default per [`§0015`](#0015--q1-resolution-candidate-b-declaredsession--operationalsession--0014-cascade-trigger-fires-q3-pre-gate-opened) with single-tier sufficiency at inception). The resolution is consistent with the [`§0003`](#0003--defer-storage-technology-selection) precedent (defer-by-property): identity tiers as a Charter-level concern are deferred; the inception-phase reference shape is committed.

- **Consequences:**
  - [`docs/ontology/entity-model.md`](../ontology/entity-model.md) §Open Modeling Questions: Q2 row (Identity tiers) moves to §Resolved Modeling Questions citing this entry. §Open Modeling Questions becomes empty (last open question closed). The Category I + Category II + Assertion sections reflect the single-tier `actor_ref` shape (revisions are minor — primarily annotation that the `actor_ref` field referenced in narrative is the inception-phase single-tier form per this entry; full multi-tier section remains text-as-introduced under §Open Modeling Questions semantics until subsequent Ontology RFC formalizes it).
  - [`docs/charter/decision-log.md`](./decision-log.md) §0023 (this entry) records the resolution; [`§0022`](#0022--implementation-pivot-64-amendment--0003-reversal-authorization--2426-posture-shift) cross-references this entry under Q2 resolution acknowledgment.
  - [`docs/charter/constitutional-charter.md`](./constitutional-charter.md) banner status line v0.4.2 patch clause cross-references this entry.
  - [`.claude/CLAUDE.md`](../../.claude/CLAUDE.md) §4 narrative paragraph appended at v0.4.2 cross-references this entry under Q2 resolution.
  - [`docs/glossary.md`](../glossary.md) unchanged (no new canonical terms warranted at inception-phase single-tier; multi-tier extension may add canonical terms when subsequent Ontology RFC formalizes it).
  - Subsequent schema-definition RFC commits carry `actor_ref` as a string field on Cat I primary observations per this resolution.
  - Subsequent multi-tier Ontology RFC (if proposed) proceeds under ordinary RFC discipline (status: draft → discussion → accepted); not constitutional amendment.

- **Supersession:** none. The Q2 forward-reference contract at [§2.3 frozen v0.4 BC4](./constitutional-charter.md#23-provenance-integrity) + [`§0017`](#0017--gate-23-second-object-level-invariant-redaction-path-a-full-redaction-amendment-v04) Resolution 4 is satisfied; the contract is closed, not superseded.
