## `0024` — Schemas technology selection: Protocol Buffers (proto3) adopted; first technology RFC per §0022 pivot

- **Status:** accepted.
- **Date:** 2026-05-19.

- **Context:** The implementation pivot at [`§0022`](#0022--implementation-pivot-64-amendment--0003-reversal-authorization--2426-posture-shift) authorized three technology RFCs (schemas, implementation language, inception-phase storage) to proceed under ordinary RFC discipline. The first — schemas technology selection — was opened at [`status: draft`](../rfcs/draft/architecture-schemas-technology-selection.md) recommending Protocol Buffers (proto3) with four alternatives considered (Apache Avro, JSON-Schema-validated JSON, Cap'n Proto, FlatBuffers). The discussion phase produced evidence at [`docs/rfcs/discussion/schemas-technology-selection-evidence.md`](../rfcs/discussion/schemas-technology-selection-evidence.md) — six Phase 4 numbered findings (F1 cross-RFC coupling, F2 hash-instability mitigation underspecified, F3 `map<K, V>` ban needed, F4 `Timestamp` vs `int64` Unix-nanos tradeoff, F5 `buf` toolchain converts AP3 to CI gate, F6 schemas-technology reversal cost) plus three Phase 3 discipline-skill applications (falsifiability-check 7 claims tested with 4 pass + 3 pass-with-caveat; epistemic-separator 5 paragraphs with 4 pass + 1 borderline; ambiguity-reducer 7 advisories all acceptable). Recommendation: accept Protobuf proto3 with eight resolution-phase modifications + four reversal conditions. This entry records the resolution.

- **Decision:** The committee adopts **Protocol Buffers (proto3)** as schemas technology for the [`schemas/`](../../schemas/) subtree per the discussion-phase recommendation, with the eight modifications enacted in the resolution-phase commit (Alternatives FC5/FC6/FC7 operationalized via reversal conditions; ES5 Motivation closing sentence split into technology-cost and procedural-cost components; AP5 hash-instability anti-pattern expanded with concrete mitigation procedure; AP6 added — `map<K, V>` ban for canonical-form-load-bearing types; F1 added to Open Questions — cross-RFC coupling with implementation-language RFC; F4 resolved in Proposal item 6 — `int64` Unix nanoseconds for canonical time fields; F5 resolved in Proposal item 7 — `buf` adopted as toolchain; F6 added to Migration — schemas-technology reversal cost) plus two committee extensions:

  1. **Cap'n Proto + FlatBuffers admissible-but-deferred registration.** Per [`§0020`](#0020--omq-2-resolution-decay-via-25-lifecycle-event-supersession-candidate-c-omq-3-cascade-triggered) OMQ #2-2 B-substrate precedent and [`§0021`](#0021--omq-3-resolution-substrate-time-generation-candidate--0020-cascade-fully-discharged) OMQ #3-2 β precedent. Both alternatives are structurally admissible (deterministic canonical serialization; union mechanisms structurally analogous to Protobuf's `oneof`); dominated at inception phase on mature-toolchain ecosystem, operational-simplicity, and §2.3 oneOf-as-wire-format-primitive alignment. Pattern: third + fourth instances of admissible-but-deferred registration after OMQ #2-2 / OMQ #3-2; the pattern is now established for technology-selection RFCs in addition to ontology resolutions. Methodologically distinct from JSON-Schema-validated-JSON rejection (rejected on canonical-serialization-fragility — structural concern, not deferred). The two registrations are governed by reversal conditions R-tech-1 (throughput pressure) and R-tech-4 (schemas-evolution pattern) per the RFC's Decision Record.

  2. **RFC procedural-divergence finding (informational; not gated on this RFC's acceptance).** Discussion-phase work surfaced two divergences between [`docs/rfcs/README.md`](../rfcs/README.md) + [`rfc-author` skill §4](../../.claude/skills/workflow/rfc-author/SKILL.md) and actual practice: (a) README claims "No RFCs have yet been accepted" — stale since [`§0007`](#0007--gate-1-pilot-4-committee-mode-redaction-path-c-narrow-but-not-minimal), [`§0010`](#0010--q2-resolution-hypothesis-subtypes-as-distinct-types-under-abstract-hypothesis), [`§0015`](#0015--q1-resolution-candidate-b-declaredsession--operationalsession--0014-cascade-trigger-fires-q3-pre-gate-opened), [`§0016`](#0016--q3-resolution-distinct-per-category-subject_ref-fields--oneofunion-exclusivity-0014-cascade-fully-discharged), [`§0020`](#0020--omq-2-resolution-decay-via-25-lifecycle-event-supersession-candidate-c-omq-3-cascade-triggered), [`§0021`](#0021--omq-3-resolution-substrate-time-generation-candidate--0020-cascade-fully-discharged) reference accepted RFCs; (b) rfc-author §4 specifies that accepted RFCs are renumbered and moved out of `docs/rfcs/draft/`, but actual practice leaves accepted RFCs in `draft/` with only the `Status:` field updated. This RFC follows actual practice (status `discussion` → `accepted`; file remains in `docs/rfcs/draft/`; no renumbering applied). The divergences are surfaced for committee resolution as separate follow-on work; not bundled with this RFC's acceptance.

- **Constitutional review:** No Charter invariant amended. Resolution respects [§2.1](../charter/constitutional-charter.md#21-observational-integrity) (Protobuf proto3 canonical wire format supports content-addressing via BLAKE3 hash of deterministic serialization; AP5 + AP6 mitigations safeguard reconstructibility), [§2.2](../charter/constitutional-charter.md#22-epistemic-separation) (Protobuf message types are nominally distinct by construction; AP1 prohibits `google.protobuf.Any` inside categorical message bodies), [§2.3 frozen v0.4](../charter/constitutional-charter.md#23-provenance-integrity) (proto3 `oneof` enforces [`§0016`](#0016--q3-resolution-distinct-per-category-subject_ref-fields--oneofunion-exclusivity-0014-cascade-fully-discharged) Q3 oneOf exclusivity at the wire format), [§2.5 frozen v0.3](../charter/constitutional-charter.md#25-hypothesis-lifecycle-explicitness) (no schemas-technology-specific requirement beyond §2.1 + §2.2 inheritance), and [§4 frozen v0.2](../charter/constitutional-charter.md#4-constitutional-design-rule) (the eight modifications operationalize comparative/judgment-bound claims per the falsifiability discipline; reversal conditions R-tech-1 through R-tech-4 supply mechanically detectable thresholds). §2.4 + §2.6 (pending — empirical pressure phase per [`§0022`](#0022--implementation-pivot-64-amendment--0003-reversal-authorization--2426-posture-shift)): Protobuf's field-number reservation + optional fields support forward-compatible additions for `influenced_by` edges (§2.4) and confidence + independence pairing (§2.6) when those invariants redact. No glossary changes (no new canonical project vocabulary; technology terminology — Protobuf, proto3, `oneof`, BLAKE3, `buf` — is technology vocabulary, not canonical per [`Q2`](../rfcs/discussion/schemas-technology-selection-evidence.md) Phase 3.3).

- **Consequences:**
  - [`docs/rfcs/draft/architecture-schemas-technology-selection.md`](../rfcs/draft/architecture-schemas-technology-selection.md) status `discussion` → `accepted`; Decision Record populated with eight modifications + two committee extensions + four reversal conditions; substantive sections updated per the eight modifications.
  - [`docs/charter/decision-log.md`](./decision-log.md) §0024 (this entry) records the resolution.
  - [`docs/rfcs/discussion/schemas-technology-selection-evidence.md`](../rfcs/discussion/schemas-technology-selection-evidence.md) preserved as historical record of the discussion phase per [`docs/rfcs/discussion/README.md`](../rfcs/discussion/README.md) discipline. Status remains "in-discussion" per the file's own convention (preserved-as-was; not retroactively updated).
  - **First non-ontology RFC accepted.** Pattern: prior accepted RFCs were charter-amendment (`v0.1.1`, `v0.2`, `v0.2.1`, `v0.3`, `v0.4`, `v0.4.1`, `v0.4.2`) or ontology-revision (Q1, Q2, Q3, Q4, OMQ #2, OMQ #3); this is the first `Type: architecture` RFC. The acceptance pattern (status flip + Decision Record populate + decision-log entry) carries over without modification.
  - **Subsequent technology RFCs proceed.** Implementation-language RFC inherits F1 — cross-RFC coupling: chosen language must have maintained `protoc` plugin AND faithful proto3 wire-format implementation. Inception-phase storage-technology RFC inherits F6 — schemas-technology reversal cost as a Migration consideration (substrate-resident hashes are part of substrate references; cross-technology migration carries §2.1-load-bearing operations).
  - **Subsequent follow-on RFC anticipated.** A schemas-evolution-discipline RFC is named in the RFC's Open Questions as follow-on work; opens when concrete evolution events surface or before the first non-trivial breaking change is contemplated, whichever comes first.
  - **Procedural-divergence carry-forward.** README staleness + rfc-author §4 renumbering-procedure divergence (per committee extension 2) noted for separate follow-on resolution; not bundled here.
  - Carry-forwards from prior cycles unaffected: Layer B follow-on RFC remains on hold per [`§0011`](#0011--q4-resolution-staged-combination-demotion-criterion-layer-a--deferred-layer-b); [`ontology.md`](../ontology/ontology.md) §Open Questions Q5 "transitive?" half remains forward-referenced per [`§0021`](#0021--omq-3-resolution-substrate-time-generation-candidate--0020-cascade-fully-discharged); [`provenance-model.md`](../ontology/provenance-model.md) OMQ #1 (Granularity) + OMQ #4 (Cross-domain) remain open.

- **Supersession:** [`§0003`](#0003--defer-storage-technology-selection) — schemas-technology portion of [`§0003`](#0003--defer-storage-technology-selection)'s deferral is now fully discharged (storage-technology + language-technology portions remain pending the respective follow-on technology RFCs). [`§0003`](#0003--defer-storage-technology-selection)'s underlying principle (technology selection subordinate to constitutional properties) is preserved; this selection enacts the principle empirically by demonstrating Charter-property-driven selection (deterministic serialization for §2.1; nominally-distinct types for §2.2; native oneOf for §2.3) over technology-feature-driven selection.

---

## `0025` — Implementation language selection: Go adopted; second technology RFC per §0022 pivot

- **Status:** accepted.
- **Date:** 2026-05-19.

- **Context:** Second of three technology RFCs authorized by [`§0022`](#0022--implementation-pivot-64-amendment--0003-reversal-authorization--2426-posture-shift) pivot. The implementation-language selection RFC was opened at [`status: draft`](../rfcs/draft/architecture-implementation-language-selection.md) recommending Go (latest stable) with four alternatives considered (Python + TypeScript/Node rejected on §2.2 compile-time-enforcement-by-default grounds; Rust + JVM languages rejected as admissible-but-deferred). Cross-RFC coupling F1 from [`§0024`](#0024--schemas-technology-selection-protocol-buffers-proto3-adopted-first-technology-rfc-per-0022-pivot) inherited: chosen language must have maintained `protoc` plugin AND faithful proto3 wire-format implementation. AP5 inheritance: pinned-library-version commitment per implementation-language toolchain commitment. The discussion phase produced evidence at [`docs/rfcs/discussion/implementation-language-selection-evidence.md`](../rfcs/discussion/implementation-language-selection-evidence.md) — six Phase 4 numbered findings (F1 `unsafe.Pointer` + `reflect.Value.UnsafePointer()` + `reflect.NewAt` as named AP1 bypass mechanisms, F2 build-reproducibility commitment via `go build -trimpath`, F3 module-proxy commitment Open Question, F4 concurrency-pattern document timing before first multi-goroutine service, F5 module-organization trigger at second-service decision, F6 cross-RFC coupling to storage-RFC) plus three Phase 3 discipline-skill applications (falsifiability-check 7 claims with 3 pass + 4 pass-with-caveat; epistemic-separator 5 pass; ambiguity-reducer 5 advisories acceptable). Recommendation: accept Go with six resolution-phase modifications + four reversal conditions. This entry records the resolution.

- **Decision:** The committee adopts **Go (latest stable release at acceptance)** as implementation language for services in [`services/`](../../services/) per the discussion-phase recommendation, with the six modifications enacted in the resolution-phase commit (FC2/FC3/FC6/FC7 operationalized via concrete verification predicates and empirical baselines; AP1 expanded with named bypass mechanisms `unsafe.Pointer` + `reflect.Value.UnsafePointer()` + `reflect.NewAt` and CI lint rule commitment; build-reproducibility commitment added as Proposal item 8 — `go build -trimpath` + `GOFLAGS="-trimpath"` + CI byte-identical verification; module-organization trigger clarified — second-service decision, not procedural cadence; concurrency-pattern document timing clarified — before first multi-goroutine service; module-proxy supply-chain-hygiene Open Question added for committee deliberation at second-non-stdlib-dependency-introduction) plus two committee extensions:

  1. **Rust + JVM languages admissible-but-deferred registration.** Per [`§0020`](#0020--omq-2-resolution-decay-via-25-lifecycle-event-supersession-candidate-c-omq-3-cascade-triggered) OMQ #2-2 B-substrate + [`§0021`](#0021--omq-3-resolution-substrate-time-generation-candidate--0020-cascade-fully-discharged) OMQ #3-2 β + [`§0024`](#0024--schemas-technology-selection-protocol-buffers-proto3-adopted-first-technology-rfc-per-0022-pivot) Cap'n Proto + FlatBuffers precedent. Both alternatives are structurally admissible (Rust enforces §2.2 separation more strictly than Go via the borrow checker + no-safe-`reflect`-bypass; JVM compile-time enforcement strong via visibility modifiers + Kotlin sealed classes — sealed classes mirror Protobuf `oneof` semantically); dominated at inception phase by Go on operational simplicity, deployment weight, and default-strong-enforcement-without-opt-in. Fifth + sixth instances of admissible-but-deferred registration. The pattern is now established at six instances across three RFCs (two ontology, two technology), spanning ontology resolutions and technology selections. Methodologically distinct from Python + TypeScript rejection (rejected on §2.2 compile-time-enforcement-by-default — structural concern, not deferred).

  2. **Procedural-divergence carry-forward continued.** The [`docs/rfcs/README.md`](../rfcs/README.md) staleness + [`rfc-author` skill §4](../../.claude/skills/workflow/rfc-author/SKILL.md) renumbering divergence first surfaced at [`§0024`](#0024--schemas-technology-selection-protocol-buffers-proto3-adopted-first-technology-rfc-per-0022-pivot) committee extension 2 remains unresolved. This RFC follows the same actual-practice pattern (status `discussion` → `accepted`; file remains in `docs/rfcs/draft/`; no renumbering applied). The carry-forward is not addressed in this RFC's acceptance scope; surfaced again here for cumulative visibility, not for new resolution. Pattern observation: the divergence has now persisted across two RFC acceptance cycles ([`§0024`](#0024--schemas-technology-selection-protocol-buffers-proto3-adopted-first-technology-rfc-per-0022-pivot) + §0025) without enacting fix — surface as standing item for committee disposition before further RFC acceptance cycles accumulate or before the third technology RFC (storage) opens.

- **Constitutional review:** No Charter invariant amended. Resolution respects [§2.1](../charter/constitutional-charter.md#21-observational-integrity) (Go's `google.golang.org/protobuf` v2 library produces bit-stable canonical bytes per [`§0024`](#0024--schemas-technology-selection-protocol-buffers-proto3-adopted-first-technology-rfc-per-0022-pivot) AP5 mitigation; build-reproducibility Proposal item 8 supplies the discrimination predicate for canonical-serialization-contract auditability), [§2.2](../charter/constitutional-charter.md#22-epistemic-separation) (Go's package-private types + unexported struct fields enforce categorical separation at compile time by default — the minimal default-strong-enforcement option in the evaluated set; AP1 expansion names the specific bypass mechanisms that would defeat the enforcement), [§2.3 frozen v0.4](../charter/constitutional-charter.md#23-provenance-integrity) (Go bindings preserve [`§0016`](#0016--q3-resolution-distinct-per-category-subject_ref-fields--oneofunion-exclusivity-0014-cascade-fully-discharged) Q3 `oneof` exclusivity at the type level via generated interface types; nil-branch discipline caveat surfaced for canonical-serialization-contract follow-on document), [§2.5 frozen v0.3](../charter/constitutional-charter.md#25-hypothesis-lifecycle-explicitness) (no language-specific requirement beyond §2.1 + §2.2 inheritance), and [§4 frozen v0.2](../charter/constitutional-charter.md#4-constitutional-design-rule) (the six modifications operationalize comparative/judgment-bound claims per the falsifiability discipline; reversal conditions R-lang-1 through R-lang-4 supply mechanically detectable thresholds). §2.4 + §2.6 (pending — empirical pressure phase per [`§0022`](#0022--implementation-pivot-64-amendment--0003-reversal-authorization--2426-posture-shift)): Go's type system accommodates forward-compatible additions when those invariants redact (optional Protobuf fields generate Go-pointer-typed fields supporting nil-checks for absence). No glossary changes (no new canonical project vocabulary; technology terminology — Go, goroutines, `google.golang.org/protobuf`, `go.mod`, `protoc-gen-go` — is technology vocabulary).

- **Consequences:**
  - [`docs/rfcs/draft/architecture-implementation-language-selection.md`](../rfcs/draft/architecture-implementation-language-selection.md) status `discussion` → `accepted`; Decision Record populated with six modifications + two committee extensions + four reversal conditions; substantive sections updated per the six modifications.
  - [`docs/charter/decision-log.md`](./decision-log.md) §0025 (this entry) records the resolution.
  - [`docs/rfcs/discussion/implementation-language-selection-evidence.md`](../rfcs/discussion/implementation-language-selection-evidence.md) preserved as historical record of the discussion phase per [`docs/rfcs/discussion/README.md`](../rfcs/discussion/README.md) discipline.
  - **Second non-ontology RFC accepted.** Pattern (status flip + Decision Record populate + decision-log entry) continues from [`§0024`](#0024--schemas-technology-selection-protocol-buffers-proto3-adopted-first-technology-rfc-per-0022-pivot) without modification.
  - **Subsequent technology RFC inheritance.** Inception-phase storage-technology RFC (third of three per [`§0022`](#0022--implementation-pivot-64-amendment--0003-reversal-authorization--2426-posture-shift)) inherits F6 — Go has mature drivers across most storage categories (SQLite, PostgreSQL, key-value, log-structured); the constraint on the storage-RFC option space is at the storage-category level, not at the specific-product-within-category level.
  - **Subsequent follow-on architecture documents anticipated.** Concurrency-pattern document (F4) must land before the first multi-goroutine service; module-organization decision (F5) triggered at second-service introduction; module-proxy commitment (F3 / F6 of Open Questions) triggered at second-non-stdlib-dependency introduction.
  - **Procedural-divergence carry-forward persisting.** README staleness + rfc-author §4 renumbering divergence now spans two RFC acceptance cycles without fix; flagged for committee disposition before further RFC cycles accumulate.
  - Carry-forwards from prior cycles unaffected: Layer B follow-on RFC remains on hold per [`§0011`](#0011--q4-resolution-staged-combination-demotion-criterion-layer-a--deferred-layer-b); [`ontology.md`](../ontology/ontology.md) §Open Questions Q5 "transitive?" half remains forward-referenced per [`§0021`](#0021--omq-3-resolution-substrate-time-generation-candidate--0020-cascade-fully-discharged); [`provenance-model.md`](../ontology/provenance-model.md) OMQ #1 + OMQ #4 remain open.

- **Supersession:** [`§0003`](#0003--defer-storage-technology-selection) — language-technology portion of [`§0003`](#0003--defer-storage-technology-selection)'s deferral is now fully discharged (storage-technology portion remains pending the third technology RFC). [`§0003`](#0003--defer-storage-technology-selection)'s underlying principle (technology selection subordinate to constitutional properties) is preserved; this selection enacts the principle empirically by demonstrating Charter-property-driven selection (compile-time §2.2 enforcement by default; canonical-serialization-contract-supporting library; build reproducibility for §2.1 auditability) over technology-feature-driven selection.

## `0026` — RFC procedural-divergence resolved: filename-based referencing codified; renumbering-with-rename rejected

- **Status:** accepted.
- **Date:** 2026-05-19.

- **Context:** The RFC procedural-divergence finding first surfaced at [`§0024`](#0024--schemas-technology-selection-protocol-buffers-proto3-adopted-first-technology-rfc-per-0022-pivot) committee extension 2 and was carried forward at [`§0025`](#0025--implementation-language-selection-go-adopted-second-technology-rfc-per-0022-pivot) committee extension 2. Two divergences observed:
  (a) [`docs/rfcs/README.md`](../rfcs/README.md) §Status of this Directory claimed "No RFCs have yet been accepted" — stale across multiple acceptance cycles: [`§0007`](#0007--gate-1-pilot-4-committee-mode-redaction-path-c-narrow-but-not-minimal), [`§0010`](#0010--q2-resolution-hypothesis-subtypes-as-distinct-types-under-abstract-hypothesis), [`§0011`](#0011--q4-resolution-staged-combination-demotion-criterion-layer-a--deferred-layer-b), [`§0012`](#0012--extend-mechanical-charter-blockquote-exemption-to-vocabulary-drift-v021), [`§0013`](#0013--gate-25-first-object-level-invariant-redaction-path-a-full-redaction-amendment-v03), [`§0015`](#0015--q1-resolution-candidate-b-declaredsession--operationalsession--0014-cascade-trigger-fires-q3-pre-gate-opened), [`§0016`](#0016--q3-resolution-distinct-per-category-subject_ref-fields--oneofunion-exclusivity-0014-cascade-fully-discharged), [`§0017`](#0017--gate-23-second-object-level-invariant-redaction-path-a-full-redaction-amendment-v04), [`§0018`](#0018--fix-frozen-section-parser-to-accept-amendment-qualified-status-cells-v041), [`§0020`](#0020--omq-2-resolution-decay-via-25-lifecycle-event-supersession-candidate-c-omq-3-cascade-triggered), [`§0021`](#0021--omq-3-resolution-substrate-time-generation-candidate--0020-cascade-fully-discharged), [`§0024`](#0024--schemas-technology-selection-protocol-buffers-proto3-adopted-first-technology-rfc-per-0022-pivot), [`§0025`](#0025--implementation-language-selection-go-adopted-second-technology-rfc-per-0022-pivot). Fourteen accepted RFCs at the time of this entry.
  (b) [`rfc-author` skill §4 "Numbering procedure"](../../.claude/skills/workflow/rfc-author/SKILL.md) specified that accepted RFCs are renamed with a sequence number and moved out of `docs/rfcs/draft/`. Actual practice across all fourteen acceptance cycles has been filename-stable + `Status:`-field-flip + `Decision Record` populate; no renumbering applied, no file movement. The committee-revealed-preference is filename-based referencing.
  The divergence persisted across two RFC acceptance cycles ([`§0024`](#0024--schemas-technology-selection-protocol-buffers-proto3-adopted-first-technology-rfc-per-0022-pivot), [`§0025`](#0025--implementation-language-selection-go-adopted-second-technology-rfc-per-0022-pivot)) without enacting fix; [`§0025`](#0025--implementation-language-selection-go-adopted-second-technology-rfc-per-0022-pivot) flagged for committee disposition before the third technology RFC (inception-phase storage-technology selection) opens. This entry records the resolution.

- **Decision:** Filename-based referencing is codified as the actual-practice convention. Renumbering-with-rename is rejected as never-followed. The convention reflects what fourteen acceptance cycles have empirically validated; aligning subordinate documents to actual practice (rather than retrofitting practice to an abandoned rule) preserves stable references in existing decision-log entries and amendments.md entries. Specifically:

  1. [`docs/rfcs/README.md`](../rfcs/README.md) §Status of this Directory rewritten — claim "No RFCs have yet been accepted" replaced with "The RFC discipline is active"; directory-structure description updated to reflect that accepted RFCs remain in `draft/`.
  2. [`docs/rfcs/README.md`](../rfcs/README.md) §RFC Lifecycle step 4 rewritten — "Moved out of `draft/`, renumbered" replaced with "`Status:` field updated to `accepted`; `Decision Record` section populated; file remains in `draft/` under its working-title filename".
  3. [`docs/rfcs/README.md`](../rfcs/README.md) §Numbering renamed to §Referencing and rewritten — RFCs are referenced by filename across their lifecycle.
  4. [`rfc-author` skill §4](../../.claude/skills/workflow/rfc-author/SKILL.md) renamed from "Numbering procedure" to "Acceptance procedure" and rewritten — procedure specifies `Status:`-field-flip + `Decision Record` populate + decision-log entry + (charter-amendment only) `amendments.md` entry. The "draft is never numbered" prohibition is preserved.
  5. No backfill renumbering applied to the fourteen accepted RFCs. Existing filename-based references in [`§0007`](#0007--gate-1-pilot-4-committee-mode-redaction-path-c-narrow-but-not-minimal) through [`§0025`](#0025--implementation-language-selection-go-adopted-second-technology-rfc-per-0022-pivot) remain stable.

- **Constitutional review:** No Charter invariant amended. No frozen-section prose modified. The change reconciles subordinate process documents ([`docs/rfcs/README.md`](../rfcs/README.md), [`rfc-author` skill §4](../../.claude/skills/workflow/rfc-author/SKILL.md)) with actual committee practice — neither document is Charter-rank. Pattern: rule reflects empirically-validated practice; practice is not retrofitted to an abandoned rule. The "never-followed rule" classification is itself a constitutional posture worth recording: a rule the committee has uniformly declined to follow across fourteen opportunities is not a rule in any operationally meaningful sense, and continuing to carry the prose costs more than the prose protects.

- **Consequences:**
  - [`docs/rfcs/README.md`](../rfcs/README.md) §Status, §RFC Lifecycle step 4, §Numbering (renamed §Referencing) updated.
  - [`.claude/skills/workflow/rfc-author/SKILL.md`](../../.claude/skills/workflow/rfc-author/SKILL.md) §4 renamed + rewritten.
  - [`docs/charter/decision-log.md`](./decision-log.md) §0026 (this entry) records the resolution.
  - **Carry-forward discharged.** [`§0024`](#0024--schemas-technology-selection-protocol-buffers-proto3-adopted-first-technology-rfc-per-0022-pivot) + [`§0025`](#0025--implementation-language-selection-go-adopted-second-technology-rfc-per-0022-pivot) committee extension 2 finding is fully resolved.
  - **Third technology RFC opens on clean procedural ground.** The inception-phase storage-technology RFC's acceptance cycle (when it lands) inherits no procedural-divergence carry-forward.
  - **Pattern observation: never-followed-rule resolution.** This is the first decision-log entry resolving a never-followed-rule finding by aligning documentation to practice rather than the reverse. The pattern is methodologically distinct from the v0.2.1 + v0.4.1 + v0.4.2 amendment cycles (which aligned infrastructure to existing documented commitment); this entry aligns documentation to existing-but-undocumented practice. Future skill / README / infrastructure prose reviews may surface analogous never-followed clauses; the pattern this entry establishes is to resolve them by alignment-to-practice when the practice has empirically validated across multiple application cycles, by alignment-to-rule when the rule's intent retains constitutional weight that practice has merely overlooked.
  - No backfill renumbering work; existing RFC filenames + decision-log references unchanged.

- **Supersession:** None at the Charter level. At the subordinate-documentation level, this entry's resolution supersedes the prior-version [`docs/rfcs/README.md`](../rfcs/README.md) §Status + §RFC Lifecycle step 4 + §Numbering prose and the prior-version [`rfc-author` skill §4](../../.claude/skills/workflow/rfc-author/SKILL.md) "Numbering procedure" prose. Per [`§0004`](#0004--charter-is-authoritative-subordinate-documents-may-evolve-under-implementation-pressure) (subordinate documents may evolve under implementation pressure), this entry is the documentary record of that evolution.

## `0027` — Inception-phase storage technology selection: SQLite + content-addressed blob-store on local filesystem adopted; third and final technology RFC per §0022 pivot; §0003 fully discharged

- **Status:** accepted.
- **Date:** 2026-05-19.

- **Context:** Third and final of three technology RFCs authorized by [`§0022`](#0022--implementation-pivot-64-amendment--0003-reversal-authorization--2426-posture-shift) pivot. The inception-phase storage-technology selection RFC was opened at [`status: draft`](../rfcs/draft/architecture-storage-technology-selection.md) recommending SQLite + content-addressed blob-store with five alternatives considered (NATS JetStream + Kafka + Parquet/S3 + PostgreSQL rejected as admissible-but-deferred; plain files + custom index rejected on reimplementation-cost grounds). Cross-RFC inheritance from [`§0024`](#0024--schemas-technology-selection-protocol-buffers-proto3-adopted-first-technology-rfc-per-0022-pivot) (Protobuf payload format + AP5 hash-instability mitigation) and [`§0025`](#0025--implementation-language-selection-go-adopted-second-technology-rfc-per-0022-pivot) (Go static-binary commitment + F6 storage-driver-ecosystem constraint — pure-Go driver `modernc.org/sqlite` required). Opens on clean procedural ground per [`§0026`](#0026--rfc-procedural-divergence-resolved-filename-based-referencing-codified-renumbering-with-rename-rejected) (procedural-divergence resolved). The discussion phase produced evidence at [`docs/rfcs/discussion/storage-technology-selection-evidence.md`](../rfcs/discussion/storage-technology-selection-evidence.md) — six Phase 4 numbered findings (F1 `PRAGMA synchronous=FULL` commitment, F2 POSIX-only inception-phase constraint, F3 single-writer constraint surfaced explicitly, F4 BLAKE3 collision-probability discussion, F5 VACUUM operational handling, F6 backup ordering — blob-store first, then SQLite) plus three Phase 3 discipline-skill applications (falsifiability-check 7 claims with 5 pass + 2 pass-with-caveat; epistemic-separator 4 pass + 1 borderline; ambiguity-reducer 6 advisories acceptable). Recommendation: accept SQLite + blob-store with eight resolution-phase modifications + four reversal conditions (R-store-4 newly surfaced in discussion). This entry records the resolution.

- **Decision:** The committee adopts **SQLite as inception-phase primary event log + content-addressed blob-store on local filesystem** as the substrate per the discussion-phase recommendation, with the eight modifications enacted in the resolution-phase commit (FC4 + FC5 operationalized via concrete verification predicates and the §0024 F6 contrast; Proposal item 1 expanded with `PRAGMA synchronous=FULL` + `PRAGMA journal_mode=WAL` commitments + CI verification; Proposal item 5 backup-ordering load-bearing — blob-store first then SQLite to ensure recovery-safe snapshot ordering; Open Questions extended with POSIX-only inception-phase constraint + single-writer explicit constraint + VACUUM operational cadence; AP4 split into §2.1-inheritance-restatement + operational-discipline-of-hash-verification-omission anti-patterns; new AP6 added on apparent-duplicate-write byte-equality verification on idempotent commits) plus two committee extensions:

  1. **Admissible-but-deferred registration pattern fully established at ten instances.** [`§0020`](#0020--omq-2-resolution-decay-via-25-lifecycle-event-supersession-candidate-c-omq-3-cascade-triggered) B-substrate (1st) + [`§0021`](#0021--omq-3-resolution-substrate-time-generation-candidate--0020-cascade-fully-discharged) β (2nd) + [`§0024`](#0024--schemas-technology-selection-protocol-buffers-proto3-adopted-first-technology-rfc-per-0022-pivot) Cap'n Proto + FlatBuffers (3rd + 4th) + [`§0025`](#0025--implementation-language-selection-go-adopted-second-technology-rfc-per-0022-pivot) Rust + JVM languages (5th + 6th) + this RFC's NATS JetStream + Kafka + Parquet/S3 + PostgreSQL (7th – 10th). The pattern spans two ontology RFCs and three technology RFCs; it is empirically established as the standard rejection-shape for structurally-sound candidates rejected on operational/inception-phase grounds. Future RFCs (architecture, follow-on technology) may invoke the pattern by name without re-deriving its rationale. Methodologically distinct from structural-permanent rejections (plain files + custom index per this RFC; JSON-Schema-validated JSON per [`§0024`](#0024--schemas-technology-selection-protocol-buffers-proto3-adopted-first-technology-rfc-per-0022-pivot); Python + TypeScript per [`§0025`](#0025--implementation-language-selection-go-adopted-second-technology-rfc-per-0022-pivot)) where the rejection has no reversal condition because the rejection is structural-and-permanent.

  2. **Final inception-phase technology gate cleared.** All three technology RFCs authorized by [`§0022`](#0022--implementation-pivot-64-amendment--0003-reversal-authorization--2426-posture-shift) are accepted under ordinary RFC discipline (per [`§0026`](#0026--rfc-procedural-divergence-resolved-filename-based-referencing-codified-renumbering-with-rename-rejected) codified procedure): [`§0024`](#0024--schemas-technology-selection-protocol-buffers-proto3-adopted-first-technology-rfc-per-0022-pivot) schemas (Protobuf proto3), [`§0025`](#0025--implementation-language-selection-go-adopted-second-technology-rfc-per-0022-pivot) language (Go), this RFC storage (SQLite + blob-store). The implementation gate per [CLAUDE.md §6.4](../../.claude/CLAUDE.md) was cleared at [`§0022`](#0022--implementation-pivot-64-amendment--0003-reversal-authorization--2426-posture-shift); the technology-selection follow-on work that [`§0022`](#0022--implementation-pivot-64-amendment--0003-reversal-authorization--2426-posture-shift) named is now fully discharged. Service skeleton work (the ingestion service originally proposed in the pivot brief) proceeds under ordinary RFC/PR discipline from this point forward. [`§0003`](#0003--defer-storage-technology-selection) is now fully discharged across all three deferred portions (schemas, language, storage); the underlying principle ("technology selection is subordinate to constitutional properties") is enacted empirically across three successive RFC acceptance cycles.

- **Constitutional review:** No Charter invariant amended. Resolution respects [§2.1](../charter/constitutional-charter.md#21-observational-integrity) (SQLite append-only application-level discipline + `PRAGMA synchronous=FULL` durability + BLAKE3 content-addressing supplies mutation-detection per [`§0024`](#0024--schemas-technology-selection-protocol-buffers-proto3-adopted-first-technology-rfc-per-0022-pivot) AP5; VACUUM is not a §2.1 violation per discussion-phase Phase 1 D1 — content-hashes are computed over Protobuf payload bytes, not SQLite file bytes), [§2.2](../charter/constitutional-charter.md#22-epistemic-separation) (shared storage infrastructure permitted per §2.2 Boundary Conditions; categorical separation preserved by Protobuf message types in payload bytes; [`§0025`](#0025--implementation-language-selection-go-adopted-second-technology-rfc-per-0022-pivot) AP1 lint rule applies to the storage-decode boundary path per Phase 1 D2 finding), [§2.3 frozen v0.4](../charter/constitutional-charter.md#23-provenance-integrity) (typed `subject_ref_*` references stored as Protobuf payload bytes; reconstructible via decode; blob-store content-addressing supplies the reference-resolvability guarantee — chain-rupture per §2.3 AP6/AP7 detectable as unresolved `payload_ref`), [§2.5 frozen v0.3](../charter/constitutional-charter.md#25-hypothesis-lifecycle-explicitness) (lifecycle events as additional `message_type` values in the events table; same §2.1 append-only discipline), and [§4 frozen v0.2](../charter/constitutional-charter.md#4-constitutional-design-rule) (the eight modifications operationalize comparative/judgment-bound claims per the falsifiability discipline; reversal conditions R-store-1 through R-store-4 supply mechanically detectable thresholds). §2.4 + §2.6 (pending — empirical pressure phase per [`§0022`](#0022--implementation-pivot-64-amendment--0003-reversal-authorization--2426-posture-shift)): future `influenced_by` edges (§2.4) and confidence + independence pairing (§2.6) accommodated by the events table without table-structure changes (Protobuf payload + `message_type` column). No glossary changes (no new canonical project vocabulary; technology terminology — SQLite, BLAKE3, content-addressed blob-store, `modernc.org/sqlite`, WAL — is technology vocabulary).

- **Consequences:**
  - [`docs/rfcs/draft/architecture-storage-technology-selection.md`](../rfcs/draft/architecture-storage-technology-selection.md) status `discussion` → `accepted`; Decision Record populated with eight modifications + two committee extensions + four reversal conditions; substantive sections updated per the eight modifications.
  - [`docs/charter/decision-log.md`](./decision-log.md) §0027 (this entry) records the resolution.
  - [`docs/rfcs/discussion/storage-technology-selection-evidence.md`](../rfcs/discussion/storage-technology-selection-evidence.md) preserved as historical record per [`docs/rfcs/discussion/README.md`](../rfcs/discussion/README.md) discipline.
  - **Third non-ontology RFC accepted; technology-selection trio complete.** Pattern (status flip + Decision Record populate + decision-log entry) continues from [`§0024`](#0024--schemas-technology-selection-protocol-buffers-proto3-adopted-first-technology-rfc-per-0022-pivot) + [`§0025`](#0025--implementation-language-selection-go-adopted-second-technology-rfc-per-0022-pivot) without modification.
  - **Implementation-gate fully discharged at the technology level.** [CLAUDE.md §6.4](../../.claude/CLAUDE.md) was cleared at [`§0022`](#0022--implementation-pivot-64-amendment--0003-reversal-authorization--2426-posture-shift); the three named follow-on technology RFCs are now accepted. Service skeleton work — the originally-proposed ingestion service per the pivot brief — proceeds under ordinary RFC/PR discipline. The first commit producing executable code is the immediate next available work.
  - **Follow-on architecture documents anticipated.** The three technology RFCs name several follow-on documents: schemas-evolution-discipline RFC (per [`§0024`](#0024--schemas-technology-selection-protocol-buffers-proto3-adopted-first-technology-rfc-per-0022-pivot) Open Questions); concurrency-pattern document — must land before first multi-goroutine service (per [`§0025`](#0025--implementation-language-selection-go-adopted-second-technology-rfc-per-0022-pivot) modification 5); module-organization RFC — triggered at second-service introduction (per [`§0025`](#0025--implementation-language-selection-go-adopted-second-technology-rfc-per-0022-pivot) modification 4); canonical-serialization-contract architecture document (per [`§0024`](#0024--schemas-technology-selection-protocol-buffers-proto3-adopted-first-technology-rfc-per-0022-pivot) AP5 mitigation step b); operational-ops document for backup cadence + VACUUM cadence (per this RFC Open Questions); module-proxy commitment Open Question — triggered at second non-stdlib dependency (per [`§0025`](#0025--implementation-language-selection-go-adopted-second-technology-rfc-per-0022-pivot) modification 6).
  - Carry-forwards from prior cycles unaffected: Layer B follow-on RFC remains on hold per [`§0011`](#0011--q4-resolution-staged-combination-demotion-criterion-layer-a--deferred-layer-b); [`ontology.md`](../ontology/ontology.md) §Open Questions Q5 "transitive?" half remains forward-referenced per [`§0021`](#0021--omq-3-resolution-substrate-time-generation-candidate--0020-cascade-fully-discharged); [`provenance-model.md`](../ontology/provenance-model.md) OMQ #1 + OMQ #4 remain open. None blocks service skeleton work.
  - **§2.4 + §2.6 empirical pressure phase becomes active.** With service implementation able to proceed, the empirical-evidence-from-implementation feedback loop that §2.4 + §2.6 redaction depends on per [`§0022`](#0022--implementation-pivot-64-amendment--0003-reversal-authorization--2426-posture-shift) posture shift can begin generating evidence. The two pending invariants remain pending until concrete questions surface from implementation.

- **Supersession:** [`§0003`](#0003--defer-storage-technology-selection) — storage-technology portion of [`§0003`](#0003--defer-storage-technology-selection)'s deferral is now fully discharged. With this entry, all three originally-deferred technology portions (schemas, language, storage) are satisfied. [`§0003`](#0003--defer-storage-technology-selection)'s underlying principle (technology selection subordinate to constitutional properties) is preserved across the discharge; the [`§0003`](#0003--defer-storage-technology-selection) deferral predicate (Charter and Ontology stable; §2.1/§2.2/§2.3/§2.5 frozen; Q1/Q3/OMQ#2/OMQ#3 resolved; Ontology Drafted) was met at [`§0022`](#0022--implementation-pivot-64-amendment--0003-reversal-authorization--2426-posture-shift); the three follow-on technology RFCs have now enacted the selections under the resulting authorization.

## `0028` — Canonical-serialization-contract architecture document introduced; §0024 AP5 step (b) discharged

- **Status:** accepted.
- **Date:** 2026-05-19.

- **Context:** [`§0024`](#0024--schemas-technology-selection-protocol-buffers-proto3-adopted-first-technology-rfc-per-0022-pivot) AP5 mitigation step (b) named a separate architecture document specifying "the canonical-serialization contract … the proto3 spec version + the library version that produces the canonical bytes." [`§0027`](#0027--inception-phase-storage-technology-selection-sqlite--content-addressed-blob-store-on-local-filesystem-adopted-third-and-final-technology-rfc-per-0022-pivot--0003-fully-discharged) Consequences enumerated this document among six anticipated follow-ons after the technology-selection trio. This entry records the document's creation and the operational commitments it codifies.

- **Decision:** [`docs/architecture/canonical-serialization-contract.md`](../architecture/canonical-serialization-contract.md) is introduced as the first non-scaffold architecture document. It specifies:

  1. **Serialization stack.** `google.golang.org/protobuf` (v2 module; v1 forbidden); canonical marshal call `proto.MarshalOptions{Deterministic: true, AllowPartial: false, UseCachedSize: false}.Marshal(msg)`; `map<K, V>` ban per [`§0024`](#0024--schemas-technology-selection-protocol-buffers-proto3-adopted-first-technology-rfc-per-0022-pivot) AP6.
  2. **Hash stack.** BLAKE3 256-bit digest via `lukechampine.com/blake3` (`blake3.Sum256(canonicalBytes)`); canonical-load-bearing encoding is lowercase hex (`fmt.Sprintf("%x", h)`).
  3. **Schemas-evolution event boundary.** Library upgrades that may alter canonical-serialization behavior, `.proto` structural changes, and `protoc-gen-go` plugin upgrades are schemas-evolution events; mechanically distinguishable via the CI golden-file gate.
  4. **CI golden-file gate.** Golden corpus at `services/<service>/testdata/canonical-corpus/`; pairs each representative message instance with expected canonical bytes + expected hash; mismatch fails CI; regeneration is explicit + committed alongside the schemas-evolution commit that produces it.
  5. **Upgrade discipline.** Five-step procedure (survey, predict, run, reconcile, commit, inform downstream).
  6. **Four anti-patterns** (marshalling outside canonical procedure; hash against non-canonical bytes; golden-file mismatch tolerated; hash encoded non-canonically in canonical contexts).

  Specific library versions are NOT pinned in this document; version pinning happens in `go.mod` when the ingestion service skeleton begins. This document specifies the libraries by module name + the procedure for pinning + the upgrade discipline.

- **Constitutional review:** No Charter invariant amended. No frozen-section prose modified. The document operationalizes existing commitments ([`§0024`](#0024--schemas-technology-selection-protocol-buffers-proto3-adopted-first-technology-rfc-per-0022-pivot) AP5, [`§0027`](#0027--inception-phase-storage-technology-selection-sqlite--content-addressed-blob-store-on-local-filesystem-adopted-third-and-final-technology-rfc-per-0022-pivot--0003-fully-discharged) AP6) into a single architectural reference. Subordinate to the Charter and Ontology per [`docs/architecture/README.md`](../architecture/README.md) Subordination clause. The document IS the falsifiability predicate for [§2.1](../charter/constitutional-charter.md#21-observational-integrity) at the substrate; without it, "content-hash recomputation on read fails to match stored identifier" (Charter §2.1 AP language) would lack a concrete predicate. No glossary changes — `canonical serialization`, `content-addressable identifier`, `golden corpus` are technology vocabulary consistent with prior usage in [`§0024`](#0024--schemas-technology-selection-protocol-buffers-proto3-adopted-first-technology-rfc-per-0022-pivot) / [`§0027`](#0027--inception-phase-storage-technology-selection-sqlite--content-addressed-blob-store-on-local-filesystem-adopted-third-and-final-technology-rfc-per-0022-pivot--0003-fully-discharged).

- **Consequences:**
  - [`docs/architecture/canonical-serialization-contract.md`](../architecture/canonical-serialization-contract.md) created.
  - [`docs/architecture/README.md`](../architecture/README.md) Document Family list extended; Status section refreshed to reflect that the Ontology has stabilized, three technology RFCs have landed, and the first non-scaffold architecture document exists.
  - [`docs/charter/decision-log.md`](./decision-log.md) §0028 (this entry) records introduction.
  - **[`§0024`](#0024--schemas-technology-selection-protocol-buffers-proto3-adopted-first-technology-rfc-per-0022-pivot) AP5 step (b) fully discharged.** The other AP5 steps remain operational: step (a) pinned-library-version is enacted in `go.mod` when the ingestion service skeleton begins; step (c) library-upgrade-as-schemas-evolution-event is now operationalized via the boundary specified in this document; step (d) CI golden-file gate is specified here, enacted when the first service's `testdata/canonical-corpus/` is populated.
  - **First non-scaffold architecture document.** Sets a precedent for the format of subsequent architecture documents that consume technology-selection commitments (e.g. concurrency-pattern document per [`§0025`](#0025--implementation-language-selection-go-adopted-second-technology-rfc-per-0022-pivot) modification 5).
  - **Service skeleton work unblocked.** The ingestion service skeleton (the originally-proposed work per the pivot brief) can now reference this document as the canonical-serialization spec; the service's `testdata/canonical-corpus/` and the marshalling call sites are committed against this contract.
  - **Follow-on document trail.** Five of the six follow-on architecture documents named in [`§0027`](#0027--inception-phase-storage-technology-selection-sqlite--content-addressed-blob-store-on-local-filesystem-adopted-third-and-final-technology-rfc-per-0022-pivot--0003-fully-discharged) Consequences remain anticipated: schemas-evolution-discipline RFC; concurrency-pattern document; module-organization RFC; operational-ops document; module-proxy commitment Open Question. Opened as concrete questions surface.

- **Supersession:** None. This entry adds an artifact and discharges step (b) of an existing commitment; no prior decision is reversed or modified.

## `0029` — Concurrency-pattern architecture document introduced; §0025 modification 5 discharged

- **Status:** accepted.
- **Date:** 2026-05-19.

- **Context:** [`§0025`](#0025--implementation-language-selection-go-adopted-second-technology-rfc-per-0022-pivot) modification 5 named a follow-on architecture document covering goroutine lifecycle, channel ownership conventions, and context propagation; specified the timing constraint "must land BEFORE the first service that uses more than a single goroutine." [`§0027`](#0027--inception-phase-storage-technology-selection-sqlite--content-addressed-blob-store-on-local-filesystem-adopted-third-and-final-technology-rfc-per-0022-pivot--0003-fully-discharged) Consequences enumerated this document among the six anticipated follow-ons. This entry records its creation and the discipline it codifies.

- **Decision:** [`docs/architecture/concurrency-pattern.md`](../architecture/concurrency-pattern.md) is introduced as the second non-scaffold architecture document. Six axes of concurrency discipline specified:

  1. **Goroutine lifecycle.** Every goroutine has a named owner, a cancellation path (`ctx.Done()` at every blocking operation), and a termination guarantee (`errgroup.Group` / `sync.WaitGroup` / equivalent). Fire-and-forget goroutines forbidden in service code. Leak prevention via `go.uber.org/goleak` in tests + race detector in CI.
  2. **Channel ownership.** Creator owns; owner closes; senders are non-owners by default; function signatures use directional types where possible. Buffered capacity requires justification; unbuffered default. Cancellation-aware send via `select` + `<-ctx.Done()` is canonical.
  3. **Context propagation.** Every I/O / blocking / goroutine-spawning function accepts `context.Context` as first parameter. Cancellation errors are NEVER swallowed (return `ctx.Err()`, not `nil`). `context.TODO()` forbidden in main-branch service code.
  4. **Substrate-writer serialization.** Ghost-Trace-specific: SQLite WAL single-writer constraint per [`§0027`](#0027--inception-phase-storage-technology-selection-sqlite--content-addressed-blob-store-on-local-filesystem-adopted-third-and-final-technology-rfc-per-0022-pivot--0003-fully-discharged) F3 enforced by single `Append` entry point with application-layer mutex (direct serialization) OR writer-goroutine + channel (concurrency-on-ingest, serialized-on-write). Read path is concurrent without restriction.
  5. **Error propagation.** Errors from goroutines via `errgroup` (canonical) or dedicated error-channel patterns. Unrecoverable errors (e.g. §2.1 hash-mismatch per [`§0027`](#0027--inception-phase-storage-technology-selection-sqlite--content-addressed-blob-store-on-local-filesystem-adopted-third-and-final-technology-rfc-per-0022-pivot--0003-fully-discharged) AP4/AP5) MUST surface to service-level shutdown; MUST NOT be retried or swallowed at the goroutine layer.
  6. **Bounded concurrency.** Unbounded goroutine spawn per incoming event/request forbidden (OOM exposure). Worker-pool pattern is canonical; worker count + channel capacity justified in service `README.md`.

  Eight anti-patterns enumerated with mechanical detectability (lint rules, race detector, CI integration tests).

- **Constitutional review:** No Charter invariant amended. No frozen-section prose modified. The document operationalizes existing commitments ([`§0025`](#0025--implementation-language-selection-go-adopted-second-technology-rfc-per-0022-pivot) modification 5; [`§0027`](#0027--inception-phase-storage-technology-selection-sqlite--content-addressed-blob-store-on-local-filesystem-adopted-third-and-final-technology-rfc-per-0022-pivot--0003-fully-discharged) F3 + AP4 + AP5) into a single architectural reference. Subordinate to the Charter and Ontology per [`docs/architecture/README.md`](../architecture/README.md) Subordination clause. Respects [§2.1](../charter/constitutional-charter.md#21-observational-integrity) (substrate-writer serialization preserves append-only commit ordering under concurrent service operation), [§2.5](../charter/constitutional-charter.md#25-hypothesis-lifecycle-explicitness) (hypothesis state as projection over lifecycle events; concurrent reads safe, writes serialize through substrate). No glossary changes — concurrency vocabulary (goroutine, channel, context, errgroup) is technology vocabulary specific to the Go runtime.

- **Consequences:**
  - [`docs/architecture/concurrency-pattern.md`](../architecture/concurrency-pattern.md) created.
  - [`docs/architecture/README.md`](../architecture/README.md) Document Family extended; Status updated to reflect two active (non-scaffold) architecture documents.
  - [`docs/charter/decision-log.md`](./decision-log.md) §0029 (this entry) records introduction.
  - **[`§0025`](#0025--implementation-language-selection-go-adopted-second-technology-rfc-per-0022-pivot) modification 5 discharged.** The timing constraint ("must land BEFORE the first service that uses more than a single goroutine") is satisfied: this document lands before any service skeleton work begins. Concurrent code authored against this document inherits the discipline; if the ingestion service is initially single-goroutine, the discipline still applies the moment a second goroutine is added.
  - **Second non-scaffold architecture document.** Establishes that the architecture-document family is now transitioning from scaffolds to active references at a measured cadence (one document per follow-on commitment as triggers fire). Four scaffold documents remain (`event-flow.md`, `replay-model.md`, `projection-model.md`, `storage-model.md`); they may transition as their respective concrete concerns surface.
  - **External dependency `golang.org/x/sync/errgroup` named.** This is the first external Go dependency named for service code (`google.golang.org/protobuf` + `modernc.org/sqlite` + `lukechampine.com/blake3` are the prior three named in [`§0024`](#0024--schemas-technology-selection-protocol-buffers-proto3-adopted-first-technology-rfc-per-0022-pivot) / [`§0027`](#0027--inception-phase-storage-technology-selection-sqlite--content-addressed-blob-store-on-local-filesystem-adopted-third-and-final-technology-rfc-per-0022-pivot--0003-fully-discharged) / [`§0028`](#0028--canonical-serialization-contract-architecture-document-introduced-0024-ap5-step-b-discharged), all bound to specific technologies). `errgroup` is a `golang.org/x/`-prefixed quasi-standard-library module; structurally adjacent to standard library, though formally external. Triggers no action under the [`§0025`](#0025--implementation-language-selection-go-adopted-second-technology-rfc-per-0022-pivot) module-proxy commitment Open Question (which triggers at "second non-stdlib dependency"); this is plausibly the second after the implicit `google.golang.org/protobuf` import — operational committee judgment call deferred to the moment the ingestion service skeleton actually imports it.
  - **Service skeleton work further unblocked.** The ingestion service can now reference both [`canonical-serialization-contract.md`](../architecture/canonical-serialization-contract.md) (for serialization + hashing) and [`concurrency-pattern.md`](../architecture/concurrency-pattern.md) (for goroutine + channel + context discipline) at first commit.
  - **Follow-on document trail.** Four of the original six follow-on architecture documents named in [`§0027`](#0027--inception-phase-storage-technology-selection-sqlite--content-addressed-blob-store-on-local-filesystem-adopted-third-and-final-technology-rfc-per-0022-pivot--0003-fully-discharged) Consequences remain anticipated: schemas-evolution-discipline RFC; module-organization RFC (triggered at second-service); operational-ops document (backup + VACUUM cadence); module-proxy commitment Open Question (triggered at second non-stdlib dependency — see paragraph above).

- **Supersession:** None. This entry adds an artifact and discharges modification 5 of [`§0025`](#0025--implementation-language-selection-go-adopted-second-technology-rfc-per-0022-pivot); no prior decision is reversed or modified.

## `0030` — Ingestion service skeleton — first commit producing executable code; §0022 originally-proposed work commenced

- **Status:** accepted.
- **Date:** 2026-05-19.

- **Context:** The ingestion service was named in the [`§0022`](#0022--implementation-pivot-64-amendment--0003-reversal-authorization--2426-posture-shift) implementation pivot's original brief as the first concrete service to exercise §2.1 empirically. [`§0027`](#0027--inception-phase-storage-technology-selection-sqlite--content-addressed-blob-store-on-local-filesystem-adopted-third-and-final-technology-rfc-per-0022-pivot--0003-fully-discharged) Consequences confirmed: "Service skeleton work — the originally-proposed ingestion service per the pivot brief — proceeds under ordinary RFC/PR discipline." The two preconditions before any executable code lands were the [`canonical-serialization-contract`](../architecture/canonical-serialization-contract.md) ([`§0028`](#0028--canonical-serialization-contract-architecture-document-introduced-0024-ap5-step-b-discharged)) and the [`concurrency-pattern`](../architecture/concurrency-pattern.md) ([`§0029`](#0029--concurrency-pattern-architecture-document-introduced-0025-modification-5-discharged)) architecture documents. Both are on main. This entry records the introduction of the ingestion service skeleton.

- **Decision:** [`services/ingestion/`](../../services/ingestion/) is introduced as the first non-documentation Go module in the repository. Structure:

  1. **Schema.** [`schemas/events/v1/declared_session.proto`](../../schemas/events/v1/declared_session.proto) — Cat I primary observation type; three fields (`declared_at int64` Unix nanoseconds per [`§0024`](#0024--schemas-technology-selection-protocol-buffers-proto3-adopted-first-technology-rfc-per-0022-pivot) modification 6, `actor_ref string` per [`§0023`](#0023--q2-resolution-inception-phase-single-tier-actor_ref-multi-tier-formalization-deferred-to-ordinary-ontology-rfc-discipline), `session_descriptor bytes`).
  2. **Package layout.** Three `internal/` packages: [`canonical`](../../services/ingestion/internal/canonical) (implements the canonical-serialization-contract), [`substrate`](../../services/ingestion/internal/substrate) (SQLite + content-addressed blob-store), [`ingest`](../../services/ingestion/internal/ingest) (typed boundary composing canonical + substrate); plus [`main.go`](../../services/ingestion/main.go) (stdin/stdout I/O loop with errgroup-coordinated shutdown).
  3. **Tests.** Each `internal/` package has unit + integration tests; `go test -race ./...` passes (verified locally at commit time — three test files, ten tests across the three packages, including hash-mismatch detection, idempotent commit, blob corruption detection).
  4. **Build mechanism.** [`Makefile`](../../services/ingestion/Makefile) provides `tools` (install `protoc-gen-go` per [`internal/tools/tools.go`](../../services/ingestion/internal/tools/tools.go) pin), `generate` (run `protoc`), `test`, `build` (`go build -trimpath` per [`§0025`](#0025--implementation-language-selection-go-adopted-second-technology-rfc-per-0022-pivot) Proposal item 8), `clean` targets.
  5. **Generated bindings not committed** per [`§0024`](#0024--schemas-technology-selection-protocol-buffers-proto3-adopted-first-technology-rfc-per-0022-pivot) AP3. [`.gitignore`](../../services/ingestion/.gitignore) excludes `internal/genproto/`, `bin/`, and runtime substrate paths (`ghost-trace.db*`, `blobs/`).
  6. **Library version pins** in [`go.mod`](../../services/ingestion/go.mod): `google.golang.org/protobuf v1.36.0` (canonical-serialization-contract); `lukechampine.com/blake3 v1.3.0` (canonical-serialization-contract); `modernc.org/sqlite v1.34.4` (substrate per [`§0027`](#0027--inception-phase-storage-technology-selection-sqlite--content-addressed-blob-store-on-local-filesystem-adopted-third-and-final-technology-rfc-per-0022-pivot--0003-fully-discharged) Proposal item 4 pure-Go driver requirement); `golang.org/x/sync v0.10.0` (errgroup per [`§0029`](#0029--concurrency-pattern-architecture-document-introduced-0025-modification-5-discharged) §Goroutine Lifecycle). Library upgrades governed by [`§0024`](#0024--schemas-technology-selection-protocol-buffers-proto3-adopted-first-technology-rfc-per-0022-pivot) AP5 + canonical-serialization-contract §Schemas-Evolution Events.

- **Constitutional review:** No Charter invariant amended. No frozen-section prose modified. The skeleton implements existing commitments. Respects [§2.1](../charter/constitutional-charter.md#21-observational-integrity) (substrate enforces application-level append-only discipline via `INSERT OR IGNORE` on `event_hash BLOB PRIMARY KEY`; content-addressing via BLAKE3 hash recomputed on every blob read with `ErrHashMismatch` raised on divergence; PRAGMA synchronous=FULL + journal_mode=WAL); [§2.2](../charter/constitutional-charter.md#22-epistemic-separation) (events table is shared infrastructure per §2.2 Boundary Conditions; categorical separation preserved at the Protobuf-message-type layer; only one type defined initially — `DeclaredSession` Cat I); [§2.3](../charter/constitutional-charter.md#23-provenance-integrity) (no Assertion records yet; `actor_ref` is a content field on Cat I per [`§0023`](#0023--q2-resolution-inception-phase-single-tier-actor_ref-multi-tier-formalization-deferred-to-ordinary-ontology-rfc-discipline)); [§2.5](../charter/constitutional-charter.md#25-hypothesis-lifecycle-explicitness) (no lifecycle events yet; structure supports their introduction as additional message types in the same events table); [§4 frozen v0.2](../charter/constitutional-charter.md#4-constitutional-design-rule) (the skeleton's anti-pattern enforcement is mechanically detectable — `INSERT OR IGNORE` semantics, hash-recomputation-on-read, blob-collision byte-equality check, single-Append-entry-point serialization). No glossary changes.

- **Consequences:**
  - [`services/ingestion/`](../../services/ingestion/) Go module created; 11 source files + Makefile + .gitignore + README.
  - [`schemas/events/v1/declared_session.proto`](../../schemas/events/v1/declared_session.proto) — first concrete schemas-resident definition. [`schemas/events/README.md`](../../schemas/events/README.md) Status updated from "Not yet defined" to "Active."
  - [`docs/charter/decision-log.md`](./decision-log.md) §0030 (this entry) records introduction.
  - **First executable code on main.** This commit transitions the project from documentation-only to documentation + executable artifacts. Test verification (`go test -race ./...`) passes at commit time: ten tests across canonical (4), substrate (6), ingest (3) packages — including hash-mismatch detection, idempotent commit, blob corruption detection, and end-to-end Append → LookupRow roundtrip.
  - **§2.1 exercised empirically.** Substrate's `Append` + `ReadBlob` paths verify content-hash consistency at both write and read; corrupted blob detection (`TestReadBlobHashMismatchOnCorruption`) demonstrates §2.1's mutation-detection commitment is mechanically falsifiable.
  - **§2.4 + §2.6 empirical-pressure phase actively generating evidence.** With service code now able to run, the empirical-evidence loop per [`§0022`](#0022--implementation-pivot-64-amendment--0003-reversal-authorization--2426-posture-shift) posture shift can begin surfacing concrete questions for §2.4 + §2.6 redaction.
  - **Cross-RFC inheritance observed in code.** [`§0024`](#0024--schemas-technology-selection-protocol-buffers-proto3-adopted-first-technology-rfc-per-0022-pivot) AP3 (no committed bindings) enforced via .gitignore; [`§0024`](#0024--schemas-technology-selection-protocol-buffers-proto3-adopted-first-technology-rfc-per-0022-pivot) AP5 (hash-instability mitigation) enforced via canonical-serialization-contract + pinned library versions; [`§0025`](#0025--implementation-language-selection-go-adopted-second-technology-rfc-per-0022-pivot) Proposal item 8 (build reproducibility) enforced via `go build -trimpath` in Makefile; [`§0027`](#0027--inception-phase-storage-technology-selection-sqlite--content-addressed-blob-store-on-local-filesystem-adopted-third-and-final-technology-rfc-per-0022-pivot--0003-fully-discharged) Proposal item 1 (PRAGMA configuration) enforced at substrate Open time; [`§0027`](#0027--inception-phase-storage-technology-selection-sqlite--content-addressed-blob-store-on-local-filesystem-adopted-third-and-final-technology-rfc-per-0022-pivot--0003-fully-discharged) AP6 (apparent-duplicate-write byte-equality) enforced in substrate.writeBlob; [`§0029`](#0029--concurrency-pattern-architecture-document-introduced-0025-modification-5-discharged) §Substrate-Writer Serialization enforced via substrate.writeMu + single Append entry point.
  - **Follow-on work surfaced.** Per the skeleton's README §Out of Scope: HTTP/gRPC interface (follow-on per [`§0025`](#0025--implementation-language-selection-go-adopted-second-technology-rfc-per-0022-pivot) Open Questions); backup/recovery automation (follow-on operational-ops document per [`§0027`](#0027--inception-phase-storage-technology-selection-sqlite--content-addressed-blob-store-on-local-filesystem-adopted-third-and-final-technology-rfc-per-0022-pivot--0003-fully-discharged) Open Questions); canonical-corpus population (CI golden-file gate per [`§0028`](#0028--canonical-serialization-contract-architecture-document-introduced-0024-ap5-step-b-discharged)); unrecoverable-error shutdown escalation (per [`concurrency-pattern.md`](../architecture/concurrency-pattern.md) §Error Propagation); additional Cat I message types beyond `DeclaredSession`.
  - **Second-non-stdlib-dependency trigger fired.** Per [`§0029`](#0029--concurrency-pattern-architecture-document-introduced-0025-modification-5-discharged) Consequences, the module-proxy commitment Open Question (per [`§0025`](#0025--implementation-language-selection-go-adopted-second-technology-rfc-per-0022-pivot) modification 6) triggers at "second non-stdlib dependency added." The ingestion service skeleton imports four non-stdlib modules at first commit (`google.golang.org/protobuf`, `lukechampine.com/blake3`, `modernc.org/sqlite`, `golang.org/x/sync`). The trigger fires; module-proxy commitment becomes a follow-on consideration when operational evidence (supply-chain auditability requirement; lockfile-drift incident) elevates it.
  - **Second-service trigger NOT yet fired.** Per [`§0029`](#0029--concurrency-pattern-architecture-document-introduced-0025-modification-5-discharged) modification 4, the module-organization RFC triggers at second-service introduction. Single service (ingestion) at this commit; trigger remains armed for when assertion-engine, replay, graph, or projections services begin.

## `0031` — Canonical corpus populated; CI golden-file gate operationalized for DeclaredSession

- **Status:** accepted.
- **Date:** 2026-05-20.

- **Context:** The CI golden-file gate per [`docs/architecture/canonical-serialization-contract.md`](../architecture/canonical-serialization-contract.md) §CI Golden-File Gate has been specified since [`§0028`](#0028--canonical-serialization-contract-architecture-document-introduced-0024-ap5-step-b-discharged) but in pre-population scaffold state: the corpus directory existed at `services/ingestion/internal/canonical/testdata/canonical-corpus/` with a README, no golden entries. Per the contract: "the CI gate is operationalized when at least one corpus entry exists." [`§0030`](#0030--ingestion-service-skeleton--first-commit-producing-executable-code-0022-originally-proposed-work-commenced) Out of Scope named corpus population as a follow-on. This entry records the population + gate activation.

- **Decision:** Two corpus entries land for the [`DeclaredSession`](../../schemas/events/v1/declared_session.proto) Cat I type, exercising the full coverage requirement (every non-trivial field; no `oneof` branches in this type):

  1. **`declared-session-minimal`** — all proto3 defaults (`{}`). Exercises proto3 default-elision; canonical bytes are zero-length; hash is the BLAKE3 digest of empty input (`af1349b9...`). Tests that the empty case is itself a discriminable canonical form.

  2. **`declared-session-typical`** — production-shaped values: `declared_at` non-zero int64, `actor_ref` non-empty string, `session_descriptor` non-empty bytes. Canonical bytes are 59 bytes; hash `0bfc6c4f...`.

  Each entry is a triple sharing a stem name: `<name>.json` (human-readable Protobuf-canonical-JSON source), `<name>.bin` (expected canonical bytes), `<name>.hash` (expected BLAKE3 lowercase-hex digest). The discovery-based test at [`services/ingestion/internal/canonical/corpus_test.go`](../../services/ingestion/internal/canonical/corpus_test.go) walks every `.json` under the corpus directory, decodes via `protojson.Unmarshal`, canonical-marshals + hashes via the package pipeline, and compares against the paired `.bin` + `.hash`. Mismatch fails the test — the schemas-evolution indicator.

  Regeneration is explicit via `make golden-corpus` (or `go test ./internal/canonical/ -run TestCanonicalCorpus -update`). Per [`canonical-serialization-contract.md`](../architecture/canonical-serialization-contract.md) §Upgrade Discipline, the five-step procedure (survey → predict → run → reconcile → commit → inform downstream) wraps regeneration; running `-update` without the surrounding discipline defeats the gate.

  `messageFactory` map registers prefix → zero-message-instance binding; longest-prefix-matching resolves corpus names to message types. Adding a new canonical-form-load-bearing type requires registering its factory plus authoring at least one `.json` entry.

- **Constitutional review:** No Charter invariant amended. No frozen-section prose modified. The corpus operationalizes existing commitments ([`§0024`](#0024--schemas-technology-selection-protocol-buffers-proto3-adopted-first-technology-rfc-per-0022-pivot) AP5 mitigation step d; [`§0028`](#0028--canonical-serialization-contract-architecture-document-introduced-0024-ap5-step-b-discharged) §CI Golden-File Gate). Respects [§2.1](../charter/constitutional-charter.md#21-observational-integrity) — the gate is the empirical falsifiability predicate for the canonical-serialization contract that underpins §2.1's content-addressable-identifier mechanism. A library-version drift that silently changes canonical serialization now fails CI rather than silently degrading §2.1's mutation-detection capability. The golden hash for the empty case (`af1349b9f5f9a1a6a0404dea36dcc9499bcb25c9adc112b7cc9a93cae41f3262`) is the well-known BLAKE3-256 digest of zero-length input — verifiable externally against the BLAKE3 reference spec; the gate is anchored at a publicly-checkable fixed point. No glossary changes.

- **Consequences:**
  - [`services/ingestion/internal/canonical/testdata/canonical-corpus/`](../../services/ingestion/internal/canonical/testdata/canonical-corpus/) populated with two entries (six new files: 2 × {`.json`, `.bin`, `.hash`}).
  - [`services/ingestion/internal/canonical/corpus_test.go`](../../services/ingestion/internal/canonical/corpus_test.go) added — discovery-based test + `-update` switch for regeneration.
  - [`services/ingestion/Makefile`](../../services/ingestion/Makefile) extended — new `golden-corpus` target.
  - [`services/ingestion/internal/canonical/testdata/canonical-corpus/README.md`](../../services/ingestion/internal/canonical/testdata/canonical-corpus/README.md) refreshed — Status moved from "pre-population scaffold" to "Active"; layout, current entries, and regeneration procedure documented.
  - **CI golden-file gate operational.** The `services/ingestion — build + test` CI job introduced at PR #2 (`f64ca44`) now exercises the gate on every push: `go test -race ./...` runs the corpus test as part of the package test suite. A library upgrade or marshalling-pipeline change that alters canonical serialization will fail the job, surfacing the schemas-evolution event mechanically.
  - **§0024 AP5 mitigation now complete across all four steps.** Step (a) pinned-library-version: enacted in `services/ingestion/go.mod` per [`§0030`](#0030--ingestion-service-skeleton--first-commit-producing-executable-code-0022-originally-proposed-work-commenced). Step (b) canonical-serialization-contract architecture document: discharged at [`§0028`](#0028--canonical-serialization-contract-architecture-document-introduced-0024-ap5-step-b-discharged). Step (c) library-upgrade-as-schemas-evolution: boundary defined in canonical-serialization-contract §Schemas-Evolution Events. Step (d) CI golden-file gate: operationalized by this entry.
  - **Follow-on work surfaced.** A `regenerate-canonical-corpus.sh` shell wrapper was named in the original corpus README but is not added in this commit — the Makefile target supersedes the need. Additional corpus variants (negative `declared_at`, large `session_descriptor`) may land in follow-on commits when coverage gaps surface empirically. Cross-message-type coverage will grow as additional Cat I / Cat II / Cat III message types are defined.

- **Supersession:** None. This entry discharges step (d) of an existing commitment from [`§0024`](#0024--schemas-technology-selection-protocol-buffers-proto3-adopted-first-technology-rfc-per-0022-pivot) AP5; no prior decision reversed.
